Proof.

Proof for priority 1-2-3/4-2-3/4-5-3 (Chen-S).

In the balanced DHV network under priority 1,2,3, we have

R_{1,2,3}^{-1}(\Delta)=\left[\begin{matrix}1&0&0\mcr\displaystyle\frac{m_{5}}{m_{4}}&1&0\mcr\displaystyle\frac{m_{6}}{m_{4}}&\displaystyle\frac{m_{6}}{m_{5}}&1\end{matrix}\right],\;\;R_{1,2,3}(\Delta)=\left[\begin{matrix}1&0&0\mcr-\displaystyle\frac{m_{5}}{m_{4}}&1&0\mcr 0&-\displaystyle\frac{m_{6}}{m_{5}}&1\end{matrix}\right],\\

where
R_{1,2,3}(\Delta)
is a lower triangular matrix whose inverse exists and is non-negative. Thus, with Lemma
7
,
R_{1,2,3}(\Delta)
is both
completely-
\mathcal{S}
and
Schur-S
, and with
\alpha_{1}<1
, it satisfies Chen-
\mathcal{S}
according to Lemma
2
.

Similarly, with

R_{4,2,3}(\Delta)^{-1}=\left[\begin{matrix}\displaystyle\frac{1}{m_{1}}&0&0\mcr\displaystyle\frac{1}{m_{1}}&1&0\mcr\displaystyle\frac{1}{m_{1}}&\displaystyle\frac{m_{6}}{m_{5}}&1\end{matrix}\right],\;\;R_{4,2,3}(\Delta)=\left[\begin{matrix}m_{1}&0&0\mcr-1&1&0\mcr\displaystyle\frac{m_{2}-m_{3}}{m_{5}}&-\displaystyle\frac{m_{6}}{m_{5}}&1\end{matrix}\right];\\

R_{4,5,3}(\Delta)^{-1}=\left[\begin{matrix}\displaystyle\frac{1}{m_{1}}&\displaystyle\frac{m_{4}}{m_{2}}&0\mcr\displaystyle\frac{1}{m_{1}}&\displaystyle\frac{1}{m_{2}}&0\mcr\displaystyle\frac{1}{m_{1}}&\displaystyle\frac{1}{m_{2}}&1\end{matrix}\right],\;\;R_{4,5,3}(\Delta)=\left[\begin{matrix}1&m_{1}-1&0\mcr-\displaystyle\frac{m_{2}}{m_{1}}&\displaystyle\frac{m_{2}}{m_{1}}&0\mcr 0&-1&1\end{matrix}\right];\\

we know that
R_{4,2,3}(\Delta)
is lower triangular matrix whose inverse exists and is non-negative;
R_{4,5,3}(\Delta)
is a non-singular
M-matrix
. Thus, with Lemma
7
and Lemma
8
,
we can conclude that
R_{4,2,3}(\Delta)
and
R_{4,5,3}(\Delta)
satisfies Chen-
\mathcal{S}
with
\alpha_{1}<1
.
∎∎

This completes the proof that the
(R,\theta)
for the 8 static-priority cases are all Chen-
\mathcal{S}
.

As stated above, the reflection matrices for the 8 balanced DHV networks following static-priority policies are all invertible. Now we check the sign of their determinants. We can calculate that
det(R_{4,2,3})=m_{1}
,
det(R_{4,5,3})=m_{2}
,
det(R_{4,5,6})=m_{3}
,
det(R_{4,2,6})=\displaystyle\frac{m_{1}m_{3}m_{5}}{m_{1}m_{3}m_{5}+m_{2}m_{4}m_{6}}
,
det(R_{1,2,3})=det(R_{1,5,3})=det(R_{1,2,6})=det(R_{1,5,6})=1
, where the subscript label indicates the classes with high priority in each station. The sign of the determinants are all positive. Thus, we can conclude that the reflection matrix for a balanced DHV network is invertible for any ratio matrix
\Delta
.

According to Theorem
4
, in a balanced DHV network with
\alpha_{1}<1
, the reflection matrix is invertible and
(R,\theta)
is Chen-
\mathcal{S}
for any ratio matrix
\Delta
.

B.2
The SSC Inequalities for Balanced DHV Networks

The requirements in Theorem
7
are satisfied if for all static priority policies, there exists a vector
h\in\mathbb{R}^{K}_{++}
and a constant
r>0
common to all static priority policies such that

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathcal{H}_{\pi}:z_{k}>0}h_{k}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{k}(t)\leq-r\text{ for any regular time }t\text{ with }\lVert\overline{Z}_{\mathcal{H}_{\pi}}(t)\rVert>0.

(B.1)

We check the 8 static priority policies for (
B.1
) of balanced DHV networks in this appendix.

Given a policy
\pi
, let
\overline{Z}^{\pi}_{k}(t)
be the fluid level of class
k
at time
t
under the policy
\pi
, and
\dot{Y}^{\pi}(t)=\sum_{k\in\mathcal{H}_{\pi}:z_{k}>0}h_{k}\dot{\overline{Z}^{\pi}_{k}}(t)
. Then, we need
\dot{Y}^{\pi}(t)<0
with a common vector
h\in\mathbb{R}^{K}_{++}
for any regular time
t
with
\lVert\overline{Z}_{\mathcal{H}_{\pi}}(t)\rVert>0
, i.e., at least one of the high-priority queues is not empty, under all the 8 static priority policies. We omit
\pi
when fixed. Note that in balanced DHV networks,
m_{k}<1
and thus
\mu_{k}>1
for all
k\in\mathcal{K}
. Denote
I_{k}(t)
as the input rate of class
k
at time
t
;
O_{k}(t)
as the output rate of class
k
at time
t
. Then,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{k}(t)=I_{k}(t)-O_{k}(t)\text{ for }k\in\mathcal{K};I_{k}(t)=O_{k-1}(t)\text{ for }k\in\mathcal{K}/\{1\};I_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}
. In this section, we use a set of high-priority classes to represent static priority policies, for example,
(4, 2, 6) stands for a static priority policy that class 4, 2 and 6 have high priorities in each station.

Case 1:
(4, 2, 6)

1.

\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{4}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{6}(t)>0.

Since class 4,2,6 are all not empty and have priorities in their stations, servers focus on class 4,2,6 jobs, and
O_{1}(t)=O_{3}(t)=O_{5}(t)=0
, which means that
I_{4}(t)=I_{2}(t)=I_{6}(t)=0
. Thus,
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{2}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)+h_{4}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)+h_{6}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)<0
, since
O_{2}(t),O_{4}(t),O_{6}(t)>0
.

2.

\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{6}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{4}(t)=0.

Since class 2 and 6 are not empty and have priorities in their stations,
O_{3}(t)=O_{5}(t)=0
. Thus,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)=0-\mu_{6}=-\mu_{6}
and
O_{2}(t)=\mu_{2}
. Since class 4 have high priority in station 1 but queue 4 is empty and
I_{4}(t)=O_{3}(t)=0
, station 1 focuses on class 1 jobs. If queue 1 is not empty,
O_{1}(t)=\mu_{1}
, and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}
; if queue 1 is empty,
O_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}
(since
\alpha_{1}<1<\mu_{1}
), and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{2}
. Thus,
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{2}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)+h_{6}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)=h_{2}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})-h_{6}\mu_{6}
or
h_{2}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{2})-h_{6}\mu_{6}
. Since
h_{2}>0,\alpha_{1}<\mu_{1}
, we have
h_{2}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})-h_{6}\mu_{6}>h_{2}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{2})-h_{6}\mu_{6}
. Thus, we need
h_{2}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})-h_{6}\mu_{6}<0
.

3.

\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{4}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{6}(t)=0.

Similarly, since class 2 and 4 are not empty and have priorities in their stations,
O_{1}(t)=O_{5}(t)=0
. Thus,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=0-\mu_{2}=-\mu_{2}
and
O_{4}(t)=\mu_{4}
. Since class 6 have high priority in station 3 but queue 6 is empty and
I_{6}(t)=0
, station 3 focuses on class 3 jobs. If queue 3 is not empty, the
O_{3}(t)=\mu_{3}
, and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=\mu_{3}-\mu_{4}
; if queue 3 is empty,
O_{3}(t)=min\{\mu_{2},\mu_{3}\}
, and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=min\{\mu_{2},\mu_{3}\}-\mu_{4}<\mu_{3}-\mu_{4}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{2}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)+h_{4}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)\leq h_{4}(\mu_{3}-\mu_{4})-h_{2}\mu_{2}<0
.

4.

\overline{Z}_{4}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{6}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{2}(t)=0.

Since class 4 and 6 are not empty and have priorities in their stations,
O_{1}(t)=O_{3}(t)=0
. Thus,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=0-\mu_{4}=-\mu_{4}
and
O_{6}(t)=\mu_{6}
. Since class 2 have high priority in station 2 but queue 2 is empty and
I_{2}(t)=0
, station 2 focuses on class 5 jobs. If queue 5 is not empty,
O_{5}(t)=\mu_{5}
, and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)=\mu_{5}-\mu_{6}
; if queue 5 is empty,
O_{5}(t)=min\{\mu_{4},\mu_{5}\}
, and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)=min\{\mu_{4},\mu_{5}\}-\mu_{6}<\mu_{5}-\mu_{6}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{4}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)+h_{6}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)\leq h_{6}(\mu_{5}-\mu_{6})-h_{4}\mu_{4}<0
.

5.

\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{4}(t)=\overline{Z}_{6}(t)=0.

We need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{2}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)<0
, which is equivalent to
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)<0
. Since class 2 have priority in station 2 and queue 2 is not empty,
O_{5}(t)=0
and
O_{2}(t)=\mu_{2}
. As queue 6 is empty,
I_{6}(t)=O_{5}(t)=0
, we know that
O_{6}(t)=0
and station 3 focus on class 3 jobs. If queue 3 is not empty,
O_{3}(t)=\mu_{3}
; if queue 3 is empty,
O_{3}(t)=min\{\mu_{2},\mu_{3}\}\leq\mu_{3}
. Thus,
\dot{\overline{T}}_{4}(t)=\frac{O_{3}(t)}{\mu_{4}}\geq\frac{min\{\mu_{2},\mu_{3}\}}{\mu_{4}}
.

Since class 4 have priority in station 1 and queue 4 is empty, we have
\mu_{4}\dot{\overline{T}}_{4}(t)=O_{3}(t)
.
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)<0
is equivalent to
O_{1}(t)=\mu_{1}\dot{\overline{T}}_{1}(t)<O_{2}(t)=\mu_{2}
. Since
0\leq\dot{\overline{T}}_{1}(t)\leq 1-\dot{\overline{T}}_{4}(t)
, it is sufficient to consider
\mu_{1}(1-\frac{min\{\mu_{2},\mu_{3}\}}{\mu_{4}})<\mu_{2}
, which requires
\mu_{1}(1-\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{4}})<\mu_{2}
and
\mu_{1}(1-\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{4}})<\mu_{2}
.

\mu_{1}(1-\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{4}})<\mu_{2}\Leftrightarrow 1-\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{4}}<\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{1}}\Leftrightarrow\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{4}}+\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{1}}>1\Leftrightarrow\frac{m_{4}}{m_{2}}+\frac{m_{1}}{m_{2}}>1\Leftrightarrow\frac{1}{m_{2}}>1
is true since
m_{2}<1
.

\mu_{1}(1-\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{4}})<\mu_{2}\Leftrightarrow 1-\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{4}}<\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{1}}\Leftrightarrow\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{1}}+\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{4}}>1\Leftrightarrow\frac{m_{1}}{m_{2}}+\frac{m_{4}}{m_{3}}>1
is true since
\frac{m_{1}}{m_{2}}+\frac{m_{4}}{m_{3}}>m_{1}+m_{4}=1
.

Thus,
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{2}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)<0
is satisfied.

6.

\overline{Z}_{4}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{2}(t)=\overline{Z}_{6}(t)=0.

We need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{4}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)<0
, which is equivalent to
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)<0
. Since class 4 have priority in station 1 and queue 4 is not empty,
O_{1}(t)=0
and
O_{4}(t)=\mu_{4}
. As queue 2 is empty,
I_{2}(t)=O_{1}(t)=0
, we know that
O_{2}(t)=0
and station 2 focus on class 5 jobs. If queue 5 is not empty,
O_{5}(t)=\mu_{5}
; if queue 5 is empty,
O_{5}(t)=min\{\mu_{4},\mu_{5}\}\leq\mu_{5}
. Thus,
\dot{\overline{T}}_{6}(t)=\frac{O_{5}(t)}{\mu_{6}}\geq\frac{min\{\mu_{4},\mu_{5}\}}{\mu_{6}}
.

Since class 6 have priority in station 3 and queue 6 is empty, we have
\mu_{6}\dot{\overline{T}}_{6}(t)=O_{5}(t)
.
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)<0
is equivalent to
O_{3}(t)=\mu_{3}\dot{\overline{T}}_{3}(t)<O_{4}(t)=\mu_{4}
. Since
0\leq\dot{\overline{T}}_{3}(t)\leq 1-\dot{\overline{T}}_{6}(t)
, it is sufficient to consider
\mu_{3}(1-\frac{min\{\mu_{4},\mu_{5}\}}{\mu_{6}})<\mu_{4}
, which requires
\mu_{3}(1-\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{6}})<\mu_{4}
and
\mu_{3}(1-\frac{\mu_{5}}{\mu_{6}})<\mu_{4}
.

\mu_{3}(1-\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{6}})<\mu_{4}\Leftrightarrow 1-\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{6}}<\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{3}}\Leftrightarrow\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{3}}+\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{6}}>1\Leftrightarrow\frac{m_{3}}{m_{4}}+\frac{m_{6}}{m_{4}}>1\Leftrightarrow\frac{1}{m_{4}}>1
is true since
m_{4}<1
.

\mu_{3}(1-\frac{\mu_{5}}{\mu_{6}})<\mu_{4}\Leftrightarrow 1-\frac{\mu_{5}}{\mu_{6}}<\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{3}}\Leftrightarrow\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{3}}+\frac{\mu_{5}}{\mu_{6}}>1\Leftrightarrow\frac{m_{3}}{m_{4}}+\frac{m_{6}}{m_{5}}>1
is true since
\frac{m_{3}}{m_{4}}+\frac{m_{6}}{m_{5}}>m_{3}+m_{6}=1
.

Thus,
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{4}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)<0
is satisfied.

7.

\overline{Z}_{6}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{2}(t)=\overline{Z}_{4}(t)=0.

We need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{6}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)<0
, which is equivalent to
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)<0
. Since class 6 have priority in station 3 and queue 6 is not empty,
O_{3}(t)=0
and
O_{6}(t)=\mu_{6}
. As queue 4 is empty,
I_{4}(t)=O_{3}(t)=0
, we know that
O_{4}(t)=0
and station 1 focus on class 1 jobs. If queue 1 is not empty,
O_{1}(t)=\mu_{1}
; if queue 1 is empty,
O_{1}(t)=min\{\alpha_{1},\mu_{1}\}=\alpha_{1}
. Thus,
\dot{\overline{T}}_{2}(t)=\frac{O_{1}(t)}{\mu_{2}}\geq\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{2}}
.

Since class 2 have priority in station 5 and queue 2 is empty, we have
\mu_{2}\dot{\overline{T}}_{2}(t)=O_{1}(t)
.
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)<0
is equivalent to
O_{5}(t)=\mu_{5}\dot{\overline{T}}_{5}(t)<O_{6}(t)=\mu_{6}
. Since
0\leq\dot{\overline{T}}_{5}(t)\leq 1-\dot{\overline{T}}_{6}(t)
, it is sufficient to consider
\mu_{5}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{2}})<\mu_{6}
.
\mu_{5}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{2}})<\mu_{6}\Leftrightarrow 1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{2}}<\frac{\mu_{6}}{\mu_{5}}\Leftrightarrow\frac{\mu_{6}}{\mu_{5}}+\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{2}}>1\Leftrightarrow\frac{m_{5}}{m_{6}}+\alpha_{1}m_{2}>1\Leftrightarrow\alpha_{1}>\frac{m_{6}-m_{5}}{m_{2}m_{6}}
.

Thus,
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{6}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)<0
is satisfied when
\alpha_{1}>\frac{m_{6}-m_{5}}{m_{2}m_{6}}
.

Note that
\frac{m_{6}-m_{5}}{m_{2}m_{6}}<1
, thus, the condition
\alpha_{1}>\frac{m_{6}-m_{5}}{m_{2}m_{6}}
does not conflict with the nominal condition(
\alpha_{1}<1
). It is clear that
\frac{m_{6}-m_{5}}{m_{2}m_{6}}<1
since
\frac{m_{6}-m_{5}}{m_{2}m_{6}}<1\Leftrightarrow m_{6}-m_{5}<m_{2}m_{6}\Leftrightarrow m_{5}>m_{6}(1-m_{2})\Leftrightarrow m_{5}>m_{6}m_{5}\Leftrightarrow m_{5}(1-m_{6})>0\Leftrightarrow m_{5}m_{3}>0
, which is true obviously.

To sum up, except for the positive condition for common vector
h
, we need the following conditions from case 1:

\displaystyle h_{2}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})-h_{6}\mu_{6}<0,

\displaystyle h_{4}(\mu_{3}-\mu_{4})-h_{2}\mu_{2}<0,

\displaystyle h_{6}(\mu_{5}-\mu_{6})-h_{4}\mu_{4}<0,

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}>\frac{m_{6}-m_{5}}{m_{2}m_{6}}.

Case 2:
(1, 2, 3)

1.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{3}(t)>0.

It is clear that
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}
,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=\mu_{2}-\mu_{3}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{1}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)+h_{2}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)+h_{3}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})+h_{2}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})+h_{3}(\mu_{2}-\mu_{3})<0
.

2.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{3}(t)=0.

It is clear that
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{1}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)+h_{2}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})+h_{2}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})<0
.

3.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{3}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{2}(t)=0.

It is clear that
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}
. Since class 2 has priority in station 2 and queue 2 is empty, we have
O_{2}(t)=min\{\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\}
. If
O_{2}(t)=\mu_{2}
,
t
is not differentiable, since it will turn to the situation that
\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0
immediately. Thus,
O_{2}(t)=\mu_{1}
. Then we can get
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=\mu_{1}-\mu_{3}
and we need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{1}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)+h_{3}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})+h_{3}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{3})<0
.

4.

\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{3}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{1}(t)=0.

Since class 1 has priority in station 1 and queue 1 is empty, we have
O_{1}(t)=min\{\mu_{1},\alpha_{1}\}=\alpha_{1}
. Then we can get
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{2}
,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=\mu_{2}-\mu_{3}
and we need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{2}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)+h_{3}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=h_{2}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{2})+h_{3}(\mu_{2}-\mu_{3})<0
.

5.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{2}(t)=\overline{Z}_{3}(t)=0.

It is clear that
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}<0
and
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{1}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)<0
.

6.

\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{1}(t)=\overline{Z}_{3}(t)=0.

Since class 1 has priority in station 1 and queue 1 is empty, we have
O_{1}(t)=min\{\mu_{1},\alpha_{1}\}=\alpha_{1}
. Thus, it is clear that
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{2}<0
and
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{2}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)<0
.

7.

\overline{Z}_{3}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{1}(t)=\overline{Z}_{2}(t)=0.

Similarly, it is clear that
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{3}<0
and
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{3}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)<0
.

To sum up, except for the positive condition for common vector
h
, we need the following conditions from case 2:

\displaystyle h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})+h_{2}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})+h_{3}(\mu_{2}-\mu_{3})<0,

\displaystyle h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})+h_{2}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})<0,

\displaystyle h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})+h_{3}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{3})<0,

\displaystyle h_{2}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{2})+h_{3}(\mu_{2}-\mu_{3})<0.

Case 3:
(4, 2, 3)

1.

\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{3}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{4}(t)>0.

Since class 4 is not empty and has high priority in station 1, thus
O_{1}(t)=0
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=0-\mu_{2}=-\mu_{2}
. It is easy to know that
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=\mu_{2}-\mu_{3}
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=\mu_{3}-\mu_{4}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{2}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)+h_{3}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)+h_{4}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=h_{3}(\mu_{2}-\mu_{3})+h_{4}(\mu_{3}-\mu_{4})-h_{2}\mu_{2}<0
.

2.

\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{3}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{4}(t)=0.

Since class 2 has priority in station 2 and queue 2 is not empty, we have
O_{2}(t)=\mu_{2}
. Similarly,
O_{3}(t)=\mu_{3}
. Then,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=\mu_{3}-\mu_{2}
. To make
t
differentiable, we have
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=\mu_{3}-\mu_{4}\dot{\overline{T}}_{4}(t)=0\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{4}(t)=\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{4}}\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{1}(t)_{max}=1-\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{4}}\Rightarrow O_{1}(t)_{max}=\mu_{1}(1-\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{4}})\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)_{max}=\mu_{1}(1-\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{4}})-\mu_{2}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)_{max}=h_{2}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)_{max}+h_{3}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=h_{2}\left[\mu_{1}(1-\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{4}})-\mu_{2}\right]+h_{3}(\mu_{2}-\mu_{3})<0
.

3.

\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{4}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{3}(t)=0.

Since class 4 has priority in station 1 and queue 4 is not empty, we have
O_{1}(t)=0
and
O_{4}(t)=\mu_{4}
. It is clear that
O_{2}(t)=\mu_{2}
. Then,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=0-\mu_{2}=-\mu_{2}
. To make
t
differentiable, we have
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=\mu_{2}-O_{3}(t)=0\Rightarrow O_{3}(t)=\mu_{2}
. Then
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=\mu_{2}-\mu_{4}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{2}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)+h_{4}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=h_{4}(\mu_{2}-\mu_{4})-h_{2}\mu_{2}<0
.

4.

\overline{Z}_{3}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{4}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{2}(t)=0.

It is clear that
O_{3}(t)=\mu_{3}
,
O_{4}(t)=\mu_{4}
, then
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=\mu_{3}-\mu_{4}
. Since class 4 has priority in station 1 and queue 4 is not empty,
O_{1}(t)=0
. Since queue 2 is empty, we have
O_{2}(t)=0\Rightarrow dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=0-\mu_{3}=-\mu_{3}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{3}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)+h_{4}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=h_{4}(\mu_{3}-\mu_{4})-h_{3}\mu_{3}<0
.

5.

\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{3}(t)=\overline{Z}_{4}(t)=0.

We need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{2}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)<0
, which is equivalent to
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)<0
. It is clear that
O_{2}(t)=\mu_{2}
. To make
t
differentiable, we have
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=\mu_{2}-O_{3}(t)=0\Rightarrow O_{3}(t)=\mu_{2}
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=\mu_{2}-\mu_{4}\dot{\overline{T}}_{4}(t)=0\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{4}(t)=\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{4}}\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{1}(t)_{max}=1-\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{4}}\Rightarrow O_{1}(t)_{max}=\mu_{1}(1-\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{4}})\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)_{max}=O_{1}(t)_{max}-\mu_{2}=\mu_{1}(1-\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{4}})-\mu_{2}
, which is needed to be negative.
\mu_{1}(1-\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{4}})-\mu_{2}<0
is true since
\mu_{1}(1-\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{4}})-\mu_{2}<0\Leftrightarrow\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{4}}+\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{1}}>1\Leftrightarrow\frac{m_{4}}{m_{2}}+\frac{m_{1}}{m_{2}}>1\Leftrightarrow\frac{1}{m_{2}}>1
, which is satisfied for sure.

6.

\overline{Z}_{3}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{4}(t)=\overline{Z}_{2}(t)=0.

We need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{3}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)<0
, which is equivalent to
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)<0
. It is clear that
O_{3}(t)=\mu_{3}
. To make
t
differentiable, we have
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=\mu_{3}-O_{4}(t)=0\Rightarrow O_{4}(t)=\mu_{4}\dot{\overline{T}}_{4}(t)=\mu_{3}\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{4}(t)=\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{4}}\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{1}(t)_{max}=1-\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{4}}\Rightarrow O_{1}(t)_{max}=\mu_{1}\dot{\overline{T}}_{1}(t)_{max}=\mu_{1}(1-\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{4}})
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=O_{1}(t)-O_{2}(t)=0\Rightarrow O_{2}(t)_{max}=O_{1}(t)_{max}=\mu_{1}(1-\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{4}})\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)_{max}=O_{2}(t)_{max}-\mu_{3}=\mu_{1}(1-\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{4}})-\mu_{3}
, which is needed to be negative.
\mu_{1}(1-\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{4}})-\mu_{3}<0
is true since
\mu_{1}(1-\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{4}})-\mu_{3}<0\Leftrightarrow\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{1}}+\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{4}}>1\Leftrightarrow\frac{m_{4}}{m_{3}}+\frac{m_{1}}{m_{3}}>1\Leftrightarrow\frac{1}{m_{3}}>1
, which is satisfied for sure.

7.

\overline{Z}_{4}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{2}(t)=\overline{Z}_{3}(t)=0.

We need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{4}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)<0
, which is equivalent to
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)<0
. Since class 4 has priority in station 1 and queue 4 is not empty, we have
O_{4}(t)=\mu_{4}
and
O_{1}(t)=0
. Since queue 2 is empty, we have
O_{2}(t)=0
. Again, since queue 3 is empty, we have
O_{3}(t)=0
. Thus,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=0-\mu_{4}<0
for sure.

To sum up, except for the positive condition for common vector
h
, we need the following conditions from case 3:

\displaystyle h_{3}(\mu_{2}-\mu_{3})+h_{4}(\mu_{3}-\mu_{4})-h_{2}\mu_{2}<0,

\displaystyle h_{2}\left[\mu_{1}(1-\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{4}})-\mu_{2}\right]+h_{3}(\mu_{2}-\mu_{3})<0,

\displaystyle h_{4}(\mu_{2}-\mu_{4})-h_{2}\mu_{2}<0,

\displaystyle h_{4}(\mu_{3}-\mu_{4})-h_{3}\mu_{3}<0.

Case 4:
(4, 5, 3)

1.

\overline{Z}_{4}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{5}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{3}(t)>0.

It is clear that
O_{3}(t)=\mu_{3}
,
O_{4}(t)=\mu_{4}
,
O_{5}(t)=\mu_{5}
,

O_{2}(t)=0
, then
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=0-\mu_{3}=-\mu_{3}
,

\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=\mu_{3}-\mu_{4}
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=\mu_{4}-\mu_{5}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{3}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)+h_{4}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)+h_{5}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=h_{4}(\mu_{3}-\mu_{4})+h_{5}(\mu_{4}-\mu_{5})-h_{3}\mu_{3}<0
.

2.

\overline{Z}_{3}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{4}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{5}(t)=0.

It is clear that
O_{3}(t)=\mu_{3}
,
O_{4}(t)=\mu_{4}
,

O_{1}(t)=0
, then
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=\mu_{3}-\mu_{4}
. To make
t
differentiable, we have
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=\mu_{4}-O_{5}(t)=0\Rightarrow O_{5}(t)=\mu_{5}\dot{\overline{T}}_{5}(t)=\mu_{4}\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{5}(t)=\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{5}}\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{2}(t)_{max}=1-\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{5}}\Rightarrow O_{2}(t)_{max}=\mu_{2}\dot{\overline{T}}_{2}(t)_{max}=\mu_{2}(1-\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{5}})\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)_{max}=O_{2}(t)_{max}-\mu_{3}=\mu_{2}(1-\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{5}})-\mu_{3}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)_{max}=h_{3}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)_{max}+h_{4}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=h_{3}\left[\mu_{2}(1-\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{5}})-\mu_{3}\right]+h_{4}(\mu_{3}-\mu_{4})<0
.

3.

\overline{Z}_{3}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{5}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{4}(t)=0.

It is clear that
O_{3}(t)=\mu_{3}
,
O_{5}(t)=\mu_{5}
,

O_{2}(t)=0
, then
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=0-\mu_{3}=-\mu_{3}
. To make
t
differentiable, we have
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=\mu_{3}-O_{4}(t)=0\Rightarrow O_{4}(t)==\mu_{3}\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=\mu_{3}-\mu_{5}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{3}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)+h_{5}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=h_{5}(\mu_{3}-\mu_{5})-h_{3}\mu_{3}<0
.

4.

\overline{Z}_{4}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{5}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{3}(t)=0.

It is clear that
O_{4}(t)=\mu_{4}
,
O_{5}(t)=\mu_{5}
,

O_{2}(t)=0
, then
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=\mu_{4}-\mu_{5}
. To make
t
differentiable, we have
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=0-O_{3}(t)=0\Rightarrow O_{3}(t)=0\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=0-\mu_{4}=-\mu_{4}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{4}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)+h_{5}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=h_{5}(\mu_{4}-\mu_{5})-h_{4}\mu_{4}<0
.

5.

\overline{Z}_{3}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{4}(t)=\overline{Z}_{5}(t)=0.

We need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{3}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)<0
, which is equivalent to
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)<0
. It is clear that
O_{3}(t)=\mu_{3}
. To make
t
differentiable, we have
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=O_{4}(t)-\mu_{3}=0\Rightarrow O_{4}(t)=\mu_{3}
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=O_{4}(t)-O_{5}(t)=0\Rightarrow O_{5}(t)=\mu_{5}\dot{\overline{T}}_{5}(t)=\mu_{3}\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{5}(t)=\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{5}}\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{2}(t)_{max}=1-\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{5}}\Rightarrow O_{2}(t)_{max}=\mu_{2}\dot{\overline{T}}_{2}(t)_{max}=\mu_{2}(1-\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{5}})\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)_{max}=O_{2}(t)_{max}-\mu_{3}=\mu_{2}(1-\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{5}})-\mu_{3}
, which is needed to be negative.
\mu_{2}(1-\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{5}})-\mu_{3}<0
is true since
\mu_{2}(1-\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{5}})-\mu_{3}<0\Leftrightarrow\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{2}}+\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{5}}>1\Leftrightarrow\frac{m_{2}}{m_{3}}+\frac{m_{5}}{m_{3}}>1\Leftrightarrow\frac{1}{m_{3}}>1
, which is satisfied for sure.

6.

\overline{Z}_{4}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{3}(t)=\overline{Z}_{5}(t)=0.

We need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{4}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)<0
, which is equivalent to
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)<0
. It is clear that
O_{4}(t)=\mu_{4}
. To make
t
differentiable, we have
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=\mu_{4}-O_{5}(t)=0\Rightarrow O_{5}(t)=\mu_{5}\dot{\overline{T}}_{5}(t)=\mu_{4}\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{5}(t)=\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{5}}\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{2}(t)_{max}=1-\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{5}}\Rightarrow O_{2}(t)_{max}=\mu_{2}\dot{\overline{T}}_{2}(t)_{max}=\mu_{2}(1-\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{5}})
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=O_{2}(t)-O_{3}(t)=0\Rightarrow O_{2}(t)_{max}=O_{3}(t)_{max}=\mu_{2}(1-\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{5}})\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)_{max}=O_{3}(t)_{max}-\mu_{4}=\mu_{2}(1-\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{5}})-\mu_{4}
, which is needed to be negative.
\mu_{2}(1-\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{5}})-\mu_{4}<0
is true since
\mu_{2}(1-\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{5}})-\mu_{4}<0\Leftrightarrow\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{2}}+\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{5}}>1\Leftrightarrow\frac{m_{2}}{m_{4}}+\frac{m_{5}}{m_{4}}>1\Leftrightarrow\frac{1}{m_{4}}>1
, which is satisfied for sure.

7.

\overline{Z}_{5}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{3}(t)=\overline{Z}_{4}(t)=0.

We need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{5}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)<0
, which is equivalent to
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)<0
. Since class 5 has priority in station 2 and queue 5 is not empty, we have
O_{5}(t)=\mu_{5}
and
O_{2}(t)=0
. Since queue 3 is empty, we have
O_{3}(t)=0
. Again, since queue 4 is empty, we have
O_{4}(t)=0
. Thus,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=0-\mu_{5}<0
for sure.

To sum up, except for the positive condition for common vector
h
, we need the following conditions from case 4:

\displaystyle h_{4}(\mu_{3}-\mu_{4})+h_{5}(\mu_{4}-\mu_{5})-h_{3}\mu_{3}<0,

\displaystyle h_{3}\left[\mu_{2}(1-\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{5}})-\mu_{3}\right]+h_{4}(\mu_{3}-\mu_{4})<0,

\displaystyle h_{5}(\mu_{3}-\mu_{5})-h_{3}\mu_{3}<0,

\displaystyle h_{5}(\mu_{4}-\mu_{5})-h_{4}\mu_{4}<0.

Case 5:
(4, 5, 6)

1.

\overline{Z}_{4}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{5}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{6}(t)>0.

It is clear that
O_{4}(t)=\mu_{4}
,
O_{5}(t)=\mu_{5}
,
O_{6}(t)=\mu_{6}
,

O_{3}(t)=0
, then
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=0-\mu_{4}=-\mu_{4}
,

\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=\mu_{4}-\mu_{5}
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)=\mu_{5}-\mu_{6}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{4}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)+h_{5}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)+h_{6}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)=h_{5}(\mu_{4}-\mu_{5})+h_{6}(\mu_{5}-\mu_{6})-h_{4}\mu_{4}<0
.

2.

\overline{Z}_{4}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{5}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{6}(t)=0.

Since class 4 has priority in station 1 and queue 4 is not empty, we have
O_{4}(t)=\mu_{4}
. Similarly,
O_{5}(t)=\mu_{5}
. Then,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=\mu_{4}-\mu_{5}
. To make
t
differentiable, we have
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)=\mu_{5}-\mu_{6}\dot{\overline{T}}_{6}(t)=0\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{6}(t)=\frac{\mu_{5}}{\mu_{6}}\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{3}(t)_{max}=1-\frac{\mu_{5}}{\mu_{6}}\Rightarrow O_{3}(t)_{max}=\mu_{3}(1-\frac{\mu_{5}}{\mu_{6}})\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)_{max}=\mu_{3}(1-\frac{\mu_{5}}{\mu_{6}})-\mu_{4}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)_{max}=h_{4}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)_{max}+h_{5}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=h_{4}\left[\mu_{3}(1-\frac{\mu_{5}}{\mu_{6}})-\mu_{4}\right]+h_{5}(\mu_{4}-\mu_{5})<0
.

3.

\overline{Z}_{4}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{6}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{5}(t)=0.

Since class 6 has priority in station 3 and queue 6 is not empty, we have
O_{3}(t)=0
and
O_{6}(t)=\mu_{6}
. It is clear that
O_{4}(t)=\mu_{4}
. Then,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=0-\mu_{4}=-\mu_{4}
. To make
t
differentiable, we have
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=\mu_{4}-O_{5}(t)=0\Rightarrow O_{5}(t)=\mu_{4}
. Then
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)=\mu_{4}-\mu_{6}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{4}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)+h_{6}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)=h_{6}(\mu_{4}-\mu_{6})-h_{4}\mu_{4}<0
.

4.

\overline{Z}_{5}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{6}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{4}(t)=0.

It is clear that
O_{5}(t)=\mu_{5}
,
O_{6}(t)=\mu_{6}
,

O_{3}(t)=0
, then
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)=\mu_{5}-\mu_{6}
. To make
t
differentiable, we have
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=0-O_{4}(t)=0\Rightarrow O_{4}(t)=0\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=0-\mu_{5}=-\mu_{5}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{5}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)+h_{6}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)=h_{6}(\mu_{5}-\mu_{6})-h_{5}\mu_{5}<0
.

5.

\overline{Z}_{4}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{5}(t)=\overline{Z}_{6}(t)=0.

We need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{4}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)<0
, which is equivalent to
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)<0
. It is clear that
O_{4}(t)=\mu_{4}
. To make
t
differentiable, we have
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=\mu_{4}-O_{5}(t)=0\Rightarrow O_{5}(t)=\mu_{4}
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)=O_{5}(t)-O_{6}(t)=0\Rightarrow O_{6}(t)=\mu_{6}\dot{\overline{T}}_{6}(t)=\mu_{4}\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{6}(t)=\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{6}}\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{3}(t)_{max}=1-\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{6}}\Rightarrow O_{3}(t)_{max}=\mu_{3}\dot{\overline{T}}_{3}(t)_{max}=\mu_{3}(1-\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{6}})\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)_{max}=O_{3}(t)_{max}-\mu_{4}=\mu_{3}(1-\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{6}})-\mu_{4}
, which is needed to be negative.
\mu_{3}(1-\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{6}})-\mu_{4}<0
is true since
\mu_{3}(1-\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{6}})-\mu_{4}<0\Leftrightarrow\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{3}}+\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{6}}>1\Leftrightarrow\frac{m_{3}}{m_{4}}+\frac{m_{6}}{m_{4}}>1\Leftrightarrow\frac{1}{m_{4}}>1
, which is satisfied for sure.

6.

\overline{Z}_{5}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{4}(t)=\overline{Z}_{6}(t)=0.

We need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{5}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)<0
, which is equivalent to
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)<0
. It is clear that
O_{5}(t)=\mu_{5}
. To make
t
differentiable, we have
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)=\mu_{5}-O_{6}(t)=0\Rightarrow O_{6}(t)=\mu_{6}\dot{\overline{T}}_{6}(t)=\mu_{5}\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{6}(t)=\frac{\mu_{5}}{\mu_{6}}\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{3}(t)_{max}=1-\frac{\mu_{5}}{\mu_{6}}\Rightarrow O_{3}(t)_{max}=\mu_{3}\dot{\overline{T}}_{3}(t)_{max}=\mu_{3}(1-\frac{\mu_{5}}{\mu_{6}})
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=O_{3}(t)-O_{4}(t)=0\Rightarrow O_{4}(t)_{max}=O_{3}(t)_{max}=\mu_{3}(1-\frac{\mu_{5}}{\mu_{6}})\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)_{max}=O_{4}(t)_{max}-\mu_{5}=\mu_{3}(1-\frac{\mu_{5}}{\mu_{6}})-\mu_{5}
, which is needed to be negative.
\mu_{3}(1-\frac{\mu_{5}}{\mu_{6}})-\mu_{5}<0
is true since
\mu_{3}(1-\frac{\mu_{5}}{\mu_{6}})-\mu_{5}<0\Leftrightarrow\frac{\mu_{5}}{\mu_{6}}+\frac{\mu_{5}}{\mu_{3}}>1\Leftrightarrow\frac{m_{3}}{m_{5}}+\frac{m_{6}}{m_{5}}>1\Leftrightarrow\frac{1}{m_{5}}>1
, which is satisfied for sure.

7.

\overline{Z}_{6}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{4}(t)=\overline{Z}_{5}(t)=0.

We need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{6}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)<0
, which is equivalent to
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)<0
. Since class 6 has priority in station 3 and queue 6 is not empty, we have
O_{6}(t)=\mu_{6}
and
O_{3}(t)=0
. Since queue 4 is empty, we have
O_{4}(t)=0
. Again, since queue 5 is empty, we have
O_{5}(t)=0
. Thus,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)=0-\mu_{6}<0
for sure.

To sum up, except for the positive condition for common vector
h
, we need the following conditions from case 5:

\displaystyle h_{5}(\mu_{4}-\mu_{5})+h_{6}(\mu_{5}-\mu_{6})-h_{4}\mu_{4}<0,

\displaystyle h_{4}\left[\mu_{3}(1-\frac{\mu_{5}}{\mu_{6}})-\mu_{4}\right]+h_{5}(\mu_{4}-\mu_{5})<0,

\displaystyle h_{6}(\mu_{4}-\mu_{6})-h_{4}\mu_{4}<0,

\displaystyle h_{6}(\mu_{5}-\mu_{6})-h_{5}\mu_{5}<0.

Case 6:
(1, 5, 6)

1.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{5}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{6}(t)>0.

It is clear that
O_{1}(t)=\mu_{1}
,
O_{5}(t)=\mu_{5}
,
O_{6}(t)=\mu_{6}
,

O_{4}(t)=0
, then
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}
,

\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=0-\mu_{5}=-\mu_{5}
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)=\mu_{5}-\mu_{6}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{1}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)+h_{5}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)+h_{6}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})+h_{6}(\mu_{5}-\mu_{6})-h_{5}\mu_{5}<0
.

2.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{5}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{6}(t)=0.

It is clear that
O_{1}(t)=\mu_{1}
,
O_{5}(t)=\mu_{5}
,

O_{4}(t)=0
, then
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}
,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=0-\mu_{5}=-\mu_{5}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{1}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)+h_{5}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})-h_{5}\mu_{5}<0
, which is obviously satisfied since
\alpha_{1}<1<\mu_{1}
.

3.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{6}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{5}(t)=0.

It is clear that
O_{1}(t)=\mu_{1}
,
O_{6}(t)=\mu_{6}
,

O_{4}(t)=0
, then
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}
. Since
O_{4}(t)=0
and queue 5 is empty, we have
O_{5}(t)=0\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)=0-\mu_{6}=-\mu_{6}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{1}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)+h_{6}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})-h_{6}\mu_{6}<0
, which is obviously satisfied since
\alpha_{1}<1<\mu_{1}
.

4.

\overline{Z}_{5}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{6}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{1}(t)=0.

Since class 6 has priority in station 3 and queue 6 is not empty, we have
O_{6}(t)=\mu_{6}
and
O_{3}(t)=0
. Similarly,
O_{5}(t)=\mu_{5}
. Then,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)=\mu_{5}-\mu_{6}
. To make
t
differentiable, we have
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}\dot{\overline{T}}_{1}(t)=0\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{1}(t)=\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}}\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{4}(t)_{max}=1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}}\Rightarrow O_{4}(t)_{max}=\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)_{max}=\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})-\mu_{5}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)_{max}=h_{5}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)_{max}+h_{6}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)=h_{5}\left[\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})-\mu_{5}\right]+h_{6}(\mu_{5}-\mu_{6})<0
.

5.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{5}(t)=\overline{Z}_{6}(t)=0.

We need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{1}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)<0
, which is equivalent to
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)<0
. It is clear that
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}<0
.

6.

\overline{Z}_{5}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{1}(t)=\overline{Z}_{6}(t)=0.

We need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{5}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)<0
, which is equivalent to
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)<0
. It is clear that
O_{5}(t)=\mu_{5}
. To make
t
differentiable, we have
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}\dot{\overline{T}}_{1}(t)=0\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{1}(t)=\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}}\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{4}(t)_{max}=1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}}\Rightarrow O_{4}(t)_{max}=\mu_{4}\dot{\overline{T}}_{4}(t)_{max}=\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)_{max}=O_{4}(t)_{max}-\mu_{5}=\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})-\mu_{5}
, which is needed to be negative.
\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})-\mu_{5}<0\Leftrightarrow 1-m_{1}\alpha_{1}<\frac{m_{4}}{m_{5}}\Leftrightarrow\alpha_{1}>\frac{m_{5}-m_{4}}{m_{1}m_{5}}
. Thus,
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{5}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)<0
is satisfied when
\alpha_{1}>\frac{m_{5}-m_{4}}{m_{1}m_{5}}
.

Note that
\frac{m_{5}-m_{4}}{m_{1}m_{5}}<1
, thus, the condition
\alpha_{1}>\frac{m_{5}-m_{4}}{m_{1}m_{5}}
does not conflict with the nominal condition(
\alpha_{1}<1
). It is clear that
\frac{m_{5}-m_{4}}{m_{1}m_{5}}<1
since
\frac{m_{5}-m_{4}}{m_{1}m_{5}}<1\Leftrightarrow m_{5}-m_{4}<m_{1}m_{5}\Leftrightarrow m_{4}>m_{5}(1-m_{1})\Leftrightarrow m_{4}>m_{5}m_{4}\Leftrightarrow m_{4}(1-m_{5})>0\Leftrightarrow m_{4}m_{2}>0
, which is true obviously.

7.

\overline{Z}_{6}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{1}(t)=\overline{Z}_{5}(t)=0.

We need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{6}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)<0
, which is equivalent to
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)<0
. It is clear that
O_{6}(t)=\mu_{6}
. To make
t
differentiable, we have
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=O_{4}(t)-O_{5}(t)=0\Rightarrow O_{5}(t)=O_{4}(t)
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}\dot{\overline{T}}_{1}(t)=0\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{1}(t)=\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}}\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{4}(t)_{max}=1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}}\Rightarrow O_{4}(t)_{max}=\mu_{4}\dot{\overline{T}}_{4}(t)_{max}=\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})\Rightarrow O_{5}(t)_{max}=O_{4}(t)_{max}=\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)_{max}=O_{5}(t)_{max}-\mu_{6}=\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})-\mu_{6}
, which is needed to be negative.
\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})-\mu_{6}<0\Leftrightarrow 1-m_{1}\alpha_{1}<\frac{m_{4}}{m_{6}}\Leftrightarrow\alpha_{1}>\frac{m_{6}-m_{4}}{m_{1}m_{6}}
. Thus,
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{6}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)<0
is satisfied when
\alpha_{1}>\frac{m_{6}-m_{4}}{m_{1}m_{6}}
.

Note that
\frac{m_{6}-m_{4}}{m_{1}m_{6}}<1
, thus, the condition
\alpha_{1}>\frac{m_{6}-m_{4}}{m_{1}m_{6}}
does not conflict with the nominal condition(
\alpha_{1}<1
). It is clear that
\frac{m_{6}-m_{4}}{m_{1}m_{6}}<1
since
\frac{m_{6}-m_{4}}{m_{1}m_{6}}<1\Leftrightarrow m_{6}-m_{4}<m_{1}m_{6}\Leftrightarrow m_{4}>m_{6}(1-m_{1})\Leftrightarrow m_{4}>m_{6}m_{4}\Leftrightarrow m_{4}(1-m_{6})>0\Leftrightarrow m_{4}m_{3}>0
, which is true obviously.

To sum up, except for the positive condition for common vector
h
, we need the following conditions from case 6:

\displaystyle h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})+h_{6}(\mu_{5}-\mu_{6})-h_{5}\mu_{5}<0,

\displaystyle h_{5}\left[\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})-\mu_{5}\right]+h_{6}(\mu_{5}-\mu_{6})<0,

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}>\frac{m_{5}-m_{4}}{m_{1}m_{5}},

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}>\frac{m_{6}-m_{4}}{m_{1}m_{6}}.

Case 7:
(1, 2, 6)

1.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{6}(t)>0.

It is clear that
O_{1}(t)=\mu_{1}
,
O_{2}(t)=\mu_{2}
,
O_{6}(t)=\mu_{6}
,

O_{5}(t)=0
, then
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}
,

\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)=0-\mu_{6}=-\mu_{6}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{1}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)+h_{2}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)+h_{6}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})+h_{2}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})-h_{6}\mu_{6}<0
.

2.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{6}(t)=0.

It is clear that
O_{1}(t)=\mu_{1}
,
O_{2}(t)=\mu_{2}
,
then
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}
,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{1}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)+h_{2}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})-h_{2}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})<0
.

3.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{6}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{2}(t)=0.

It is clear that
O_{1}(t)=\mu_{1}
,
O_{6}(t)=\mu_{6}
,

O_{4}(t)=0
, then
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}
. To make
t
differentiable, we have
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=\mu_{1}-O_{2}(t)=\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}\dot{\overline{T}}_{2}(t)=0\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{2}(t)=\frac{\mu_{1}}{\mu_{2}}\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{5}(t)_{max}=1-\frac{\mu_{1}}{\mu_{2}}\Rightarrow O_{5}(t)_{max}=\mu_{5}\dot{\overline{T}}_{5}(t)_{max}=\mu_{5}(1-\frac{\mu_{1}}{\mu_{2}})\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)_{max}=O_{5}(t)_{max}-\mu_{6}=\mu_{5}(1-\frac{\mu_{1}}{\mu_{2}})-\mu_{6}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)_{max}=h_{1}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)+h_{6}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)_{max}=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})+h_{6}\left[\mu_{5}(1-\frac{\mu_{1}}{\mu_{2}})-\mu_{6}\right]<0
, which is true since
\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}
is obviously negative, and we can prove that
\mu_{5}(1-\frac{\mu_{1}}{\mu_{2}})-\mu_{6}<0
as the following. If
1-\frac{\mu_{1}}{\mu_{2}}\leq 0
, then
\mu_{5}(1-\frac{\mu_{1}}{\mu_{2}})-\mu_{6}<0
for sure; if
1-\frac{\mu_{1}}{\mu_{2}}>0
, then
\mu_{5}(1-\frac{\mu_{1}}{\mu_{2}})-\mu_{6}<0\Leftrightarrow\frac{\mu_{1}}{\mu_{2}}+\frac{\mu_{6}}{\mu_{5}}>1\Leftrightarrow\frac{m_{2}}{m_{1}}+\frac{m_{5}}{m_{6}}>1
, which is satisfied since
\frac{m_{2}}{m_{1}}+\frac{m_{5}}{m_{6}}>m_{5}+m_{2}=1
.

4.

\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{6}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{1}(t)=0.

It is clear that
O_{2}(t)=\mu_{2}
,
O_{6}(t)=\mu_{6}
,

O_{5}(t)=0
, then
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)=0-\mu_{6}=-\mu_{6}
. To make
t
differentiable, we have
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-O_{1}(t)=0\Rightarrow O_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{2}
. It obviously holds that
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{2}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)+h_{6}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)=h_{2}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{2})-h_{6}\mu_{6}<0
as required.

5.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{2}(t)=\overline{Z}_{6}(t)=0.

We need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{1}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)<0
, which is equivalent to
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)<0
. It is clear that
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}<0
.

6.

\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{1}(t)=\overline{Z}_{6}(t)=0.

We need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{2}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)<0
, which is equivalent to
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)<0
. It is clear that
O_{2}(t)=\mu_{2}
. To make
t
differentiable, we have
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-O_{1}(t)=0\Rightarrow O_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}
. Then,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{2}<0
for sure.

7.

\overline{Z}_{6}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{1}(t)=\overline{Z}_{2}(t)=0.

We need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{6}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)<0
, which is equivalent to
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)<0
. It is clear that
O_{6}(t)=\mu_{6}
. To make
t
differentiable, we have
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-O_{1}(t)=0\Rightarrow O_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=\alpha_{1}-O_{2}(t)=0\Rightarrow O_{2}(t)=\mu_{2}\dot{\overline{T}}_{2}(t)=\alpha_{1}\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{2}(t)=\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{2}}\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{5}(t)_{max}=1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{2}}\Rightarrow O_{5}(t)_{max}=\mu_{5}\dot{\overline{T}}_{5}(t)_{max}=\mu_{5}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{2}})\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)_{max}=O_{5}(t)_{max}-\mu_{6}=\mu_{5}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{2}})-\mu_{6}
, which is needed to be negative.
\mu_{5}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{2}})-\mu_{6}<0\Leftrightarrow 1-m_{2}\alpha_{1}<\frac{m_{5}}{m_{6}}\Leftrightarrow\alpha_{1}>\frac{m_{6}-m_{5}}{m_{2}m_{6}}
. Thus,
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{6}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{6}(t)<0
is satisfied when
\alpha_{1}>\frac{m_{6}-m_{5}}{m_{2}m_{6}}
.

We state in the analysis of case 1 (
\overline{Z}_{6}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{2}(t)=\overline{Z}_{4}(t)=0
) that
\frac{m_{6}-m_{5}}{m_{2}m_{6}}<1
, thus, the condition
\alpha_{1}>\frac{m_{6}-m_{5}}{m_{2}m_{6}}
does not conflict with the nominal condition(
\alpha_{1}<1
).

To sum up, except for the positive condition for common vector
h
, we need the following conditions from case 7:

\displaystyle h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})+h_{2}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})-h_{6}\mu_{6}<0,

\displaystyle h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})+h_{2}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})<0,

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}>\frac{m_{6}-m_{5}}{m_{2}m_{6}}.

Case 8:
(1, 5, 3)

1.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{3}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{5}(t)>0.

It is clear that
O_{1}(t)=\mu_{1}
,
O_{3}(t)=\mu_{3}
,
O_{5}(t)=\mu_{5}
,

O_{4}(t)=0
,
O_{2}(t)=0
, then
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}
,

\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=0-\mu_{3}=-\mu_{3}
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=0-\mu_{5}=-\mu_{5}
. It obviously holds that
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{1}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)+h_{3}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)+h_{5}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})-h_{3}\mu_{3}-h_{5}\mu_{5}<0
as required.

2.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{3}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{5}(t)=0.

It is clear that
O_{1}(t)=\mu_{1}
,
O_{3}(t)=\mu_{3}
,

O_{4}(t)=0
, then
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}
. Since queue 5 is empty and
O_{4}(t)=0
, we have
O_{5}(t)=0\Rightarrow O_{2}(t)_{max}=\mu_{2}\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)_{max}=O_{2}(t)_{max}-\mu_{3}=\mu_{2}-\mu_{3}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)_{max}=h_{1}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)+h_{3}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)_{max}=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})+h_{3}(\mu_{2}-\mu_{3})<0
.

3.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{5}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{3}(t)=0.

It is clear that
O_{1}(t)=\mu_{1}
,
O_{5}(t)=\mu_{5}
,

O_{4}(t)=0
, then
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}
,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=0-\mu_{5}=-\mu_{5}
. It obviously holds that
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{1}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)+h_{5}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})-h_{5}\mu_{5}<0
as required.

4.

\overline{Z}_{3}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{5}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{1}(t)=0.

It is clear that
O_{3}(t)=\mu_{3}
,
O_{5}(t)=\mu_{5}
,

O_{2}(t)=0
, then
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=0-\mu_{3}=-\mu_{3}
. To make
t
differentiable, we have
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-O_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}\dot{\overline{T}}_{1}(t)=0\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{1}(t)=\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}}\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{4}(t)_{max}=1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}}\Rightarrow O_{4}(t)_{max}=\mu_{4}\dot{\overline{T}}_{4}(t)_{max}=\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)_{max}=O_{4}(t)_{max}-\mu_{5}=\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})-\mu_{5}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)_{max}=h_{3}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)+h_{5}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)_{max}=h_{5}\left[\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})-\mu_{5}\right]-h_{3}\mu_{3}<0
.

In the analysis of case 6 (
\overline{Z}_{5}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{1}(t)=\overline{Z}_{6}(t)=0
), we need the condition
\alpha_{1}>\frac{m_{5}-m_{4}}{m_{1}m_{5}}
, with which

\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})-\mu_{5}<0
. Thus, without adding more conditions, we have
\dot{Y}(t)_{max}=h_{5}\left[\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})-\mu_{5}\right]-h_{3}\mu_{3}<0
.

5.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{3}(t)=\overline{Z}_{5}(t)=0.

We need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{1}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)<0
, which is equivalent to
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)<0
. It is clear that
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}<0
.

6.

\overline{Z}_{3}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{1}(t)=\overline{Z}_{5}(t)=0.

We need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{3}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)<0
, which is equivalent to
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)<0
. It is clear that
O_{3}(t)=\mu_{3}
. To make
t
differentiable, we have
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-O_{1}(t)=0\Rightarrow O_{1}(t)=\mu_{1}\dot{\overline{T}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{1}(t)=\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}}
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=O_{4}(t)-O_{5}(t)=0\Rightarrow O_{5}(t)=O_{4}(t)\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{5}(t)=\frac{O_{5}(t)}{\mu_{5}}=\frac{O_{4}(t)}{\mu_{5}}\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{5}(t)_{min}=\frac{O_{4}(t)_{min}}{\mu_{5}}\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{2}(t)_{max}=1-\dot{\overline{T}}_{5}(t)_{min}=1-\frac{O_{4}(t)_{min}}{\mu_{5}}
. Then
O_{2}(t)_{max}=\mu_{2}\dot{\overline{T}}_{2}(t)_{max}=\mu_{2}(1-\frac{O_{4}(t)_{min}}{\mu_{5}})
. If queue 4 is not empty,
O_{4}(t)=\mu_{4}(1-\dot{\overline{T}}_{1}(t))=\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})
; if queue 4 is empty,
O_{4}(t)=min\{\mu_{3},\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})\}
. Thus,
O_{4}(t)_{min}=\mu_{3}
or
\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})
, and
O_{2}(t)_{max}=\mu_{2}(1-\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{5}})
or
\mu_{2}(1-\frac{\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})}{\mu_{5}})
. Then,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)<0
is equivalent to
\mu_{2}(1-\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{5}})-\mu_{3}<0
and
\mu_{2}\left[1-\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{5}}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})\right]-\mu_{3}<0
.

\mu_{2}(1-\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{5}})-\mu_{3}<0
is satisfied since
\mu_{2}(1-\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{5}})-\mu_{3}<0\Leftrightarrow\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{2}}+\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{5}}>1\Leftrightarrow\frac{m_{2}}{m_{3}}+\frac{m_{5}}{m_{3}}>1\Leftrightarrow\frac{1}{m_{3}}>1
, which is true obviously.

Let
f(\alpha_{1})=\mu_{2}\left[1-\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{5}}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})\right]-\mu_{3}
. Then
\dot{f}(\alpha_{1})=\frac{\mu_{2}\mu_{4}}{\mu_{5}\mu_{1}}>0
. We know that
f(1)=\mu_{2}\left[1-\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{5}}(1-\frac{1}{\mu_{1}})\right]-\mu_{3}=\mu_{2}\left[1-\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{5}}(1-m_{1})\right]-\mu_{3}=\mu_{2}(1-\frac{m_{5}}{m_{4}}\times m_{4})-\mu_{3}=\mu_{2}(1-m_{5})-\mu_{3}=1-\mu_{3}<0
. So, for any
\alpha_{1}<1
, we have
f(\alpha_{1})<f(1)<0
, which means
\mu_{2}\left[1-\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{5}}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})\right]-\mu_{3}<0
.

7.

\overline{Z}_{5}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{1}(t)=\overline{Z}_{3}(t)=0.

We need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{5}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)<0
, which is equivalent to
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)<0
. It is clear that
O_{5}(t)=\mu_{5}
. To make
t
differentiable, we have
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-O_{1}(t)=0\Rightarrow O_{1}(t)=\mu_{1}\dot{\overline{T}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{1}(t)=\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}}\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{4}(t)_{max}=1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}}\Rightarrow O_{4}(t)_{max}=\mu_{4}\dot{\overline{T}}_{4}(t)_{max}=\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)_{max}=O_{4}(t)_{max}-\mu_{5}=\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})-\mu_{5}
, which is needed to be negative.
\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})-\mu_{5}<0\Leftrightarrow 1-m_{1}\alpha_{1}<\frac{m_{4}}{m_{5}}\Leftrightarrow\alpha_{1}>\frac{m_{5}-m_{4}}{m_{1}m_{5}}
. Thus,
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{5}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)<0
is satisfied when
\alpha_{1}>\frac{m_{5}-m_{4}}{m_{1}m_{5}}
.

We state in the analysis of case 6 (
\overline{Z}_{5}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{1}(t)=\overline{Z}_{6}(t)=0
) that
\frac{m_{5}-m_{4}}{m_{1}m_{5}}<1
, thus, the condition
\alpha_{1}>\frac{m_{5}-m_{4}}{m_{1}m_{5}}
does not conflict with the nominal condition(
\alpha_{1}<1
).

To sum up, except for the positive condition for common vector
h
, we need the following condition from case 8:

\displaystyle h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})+h_{3}(\mu_{2}-\mu_{3})<0,

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}>\frac{m_{5}-m_{4}}{m_{1}m_{5}}.

B.3
Sufficient Conditions for SSC of Balanced DHV Networks

The question of whether there exists such a vector
h
satisfying the 23 inequalities that we mentioned in §
4.1
can be turned into a LP problem
(P_{h})
.
If
(P_{h})
has a strictly positive solution, then a vector
h
that satisfies all 23 inequalities exists. The inequalities below are reordered by their algebraic form.

\displaystyle(P_{h})=\;

max

\displaystyle p

s.t.

\displaystyle h_{1},h_{2},h_{3},h_{4},h_{5},h_{6}\geq p

\displaystyle h_{2}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})-h_{6}\mu_{6}\leq-p

\displaystyle h_{4}(\mu_{3}-\mu_{4})-h_{2}\mu_{2}\leq-p

\displaystyle h_{6}(\mu_{5}-\mu_{6})-h_{4}\mu_{4}\leq-p

\displaystyle h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})+h_{3}(\mu_{2}-\mu_{3})\leq-p

\displaystyle h_{3}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{3})+h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})\leq-p

\displaystyle h_{4}(\mu_{2}-\mu_{4})-h_{2}\mu_{2}\leq-p

\displaystyle h_{5}(\mu_{3}-\mu_{5})-h_{3}\mu_{3}\leq-p

\displaystyle h_{6}(\mu_{4}-\mu_{6})-h_{4}\mu_{4}\leq-p

\displaystyle h_{3}(\mu_{2}-\mu_{3})+h_{2}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{2})\leq-p

\displaystyle h_{4}(\mu_{3}-\mu_{4})-h_{3}\mu_{3}\leq-p

\displaystyle h_{5}(\mu_{4}-\mu_{5})-h_{4}\mu_{4}\leq-p

\displaystyle h_{6}(\mu_{5}-\mu_{6})-h_{5}\mu_{5}\leq-p

\displaystyle h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})+h_{2}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\leq-p

\displaystyle h_{2}\left[\mu_{1}(1-\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{4}})-\mu_{2}\right]+h_{3}(\mu_{2}-\mu_{3})\leq-p

\displaystyle h_{3}\left[\mu_{2}(1-\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{5}})-\mu_{3}\right]+h_{4}(\mu_{3}-\mu_{4})\leq-p

\displaystyle h_{4}\left[\mu_{3}(1-\frac{\mu_{5}}{\mu_{6}})-\mu_{4}\right]+h_{5}(\mu_{4}-\mu_{5})\leq-p

\displaystyle h_{5}\left[\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})-\mu_{5}\right]+h_{6}(\mu_{5}-\mu_{6})\leq-p

\displaystyle h_{2}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})+h_{3}(\mu_{2}-\mu_{3})+h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})\leq-p

\displaystyle h_{3}(\mu_{2}-\mu_{3})+h_{4}(\mu_{3}-\mu_{4})-h_{2}\mu_{2}\leq-p

\displaystyle h_{4}(\mu_{3}-\mu_{4})+h_{5}(\mu_{4}-\mu_{5})-h_{3}\mu_{3}\leq-p

\displaystyle h_{5}(\mu_{4}-\mu_{5})+h_{6}(\mu_{5}-\mu_{6})-h_{4}\mu_{4}\leq-p

\displaystyle h_{6}(\mu_{5}-\mu_{6})+h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})-h_{5}\mu_{5}\leq-p

\displaystyle p\leq 1

The constraint
p\leq 1
guarantees that the objective value is bounded from above, without which, if (
P_{h}
) has a positive solution, then by scaling both
h
and
p
, this solution is infinite. We care only about whether a positive solution to (
P_{h}
) exists.

One obvious feasible solution to
(P_{h})
is
h_{1}=h_{2}=h_{3}=h_{4}=h_{5}=h_{6}=p=0
, with a corresponding objective value of 0. Since
(P_{h})
is a maximization problem, the optimal value of
(P_{h})
is non-negative. If the optimal value is strictly positive, then we can scale
h
and
p
to make it larger. Thus, the optimal value of
(P_{h})
is always
0
or
1
. According to strong duality, the dual optimal of
(P_{h})
is also
0
or
1
; call the dual problem as
(D_{h})
.

\displaystyle(D_{h})=\max\hskip 8.50012pt

\displaystyle x_{29}

s.t.

\displaystyle-x_{1}+(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})(x_{10}+x_{11}+x_{19}+x_{24}+x_{28})=0

(B.2)

\displaystyle-x_{2}+(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})(x_{7}+x_{19}+x_{24})-\mu_{2}(x_{8}+x_{12}+x_{25})+(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{2})x_{15}+\left[\mu_{1}(1-\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{4}})-\mu_{2}\right]x_{20}=0

(B.3)

\displaystyle-x_{3}+(\mu_{1}-\mu_{3})x_{11}+(\mu_{2}-\mu_{3})(x_{10}+x_{15}+x_{20}+x_{24}+x_{25})-\mu_{3}(x_{13}+x_{16}+x_{26})

(B.4)

\displaystyle+\left[\mu_{2}(1-\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{5}})-\mu_{3}\right]x_{21}=0

(B.5)

\displaystyle-x_{4}+(\mu_{3}-\mu_{4})(x_{8}+x_{16}+x_{21}+x_{25}+x_{26})-\mu_{4}(x_{9}+x_{14}+x_{17}+x_{27})+(\mu_{2}-\mu_{4})x_{12}

(B.6)

\displaystyle+\left[\mu_{3}(1-\frac{\mu_{5}}{\mu_{6}})-\mu_{4}\right]x_{22}=0

(B.7)

\displaystyle-x_{5}+(\mu_{3}-\mu_{5})x_{13}+(\mu_{4}-\mu_{5})(x_{17}+x_{22}+x_{26}+x_{27})-\mu_{5}(x_{18}+x_{28})+\left[\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})-\mu_{5}\right]x_{23}=0

(B.8)

\displaystyle-x_{6}+(\mu_{4}-\mu_{6})x_{14}+(\mu_{5}-\mu_{6})(x_{9}+x_{18}+x_{23}+x_{27}+x_{28})-\mu_{6}x_{7}=0

(B.9)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{29}x_{i}=1

(B.10)

\displaystyle x_{1},x_{2},...,x_{29}\geq 0

(B.11)

If the optimal value of of
(D_{h})
is
0
, it is clear that
x_{29}=0
. If the optimal value is
1
, with constraints (
B.10
) and (
B.11
),
x_{29}=1
,
x_{1}=x_{2}=...=x_{28}=0
. This means that if
(D_{h})
has
x_{29}=1
,
x_{1}=x_{2}=...=x_{28}=0
as its optimal solution, then the vector
h
that satisfies all the 23 inequalities exists.

Let us now consider the signs of coefficients of the variables in
(D_{h})
.
We have

\displaystyle\frac{m_{1}}{m_{2}}+\frac{m_{4}}{m_{3}}>m_{1}+m_{4}=1

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\;

\displaystyle\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{1}}+\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{4}}>1

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\;

\displaystyle 1-\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{4}}<\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{1}}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\;

\displaystyle\mu_{1}(1-\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{4}})-\mu_{2}<0.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\mu_{2}(1-\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{5}})-\mu_{3}<0,

\displaystyle\mu_{3}(1-\frac{\mu_{5}}{\mu_{6}})-\mu_{4}<0,

\displaystyle\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})-\mu_{5}<0.

Form constraints (
B.2
) and (
B.11
), since
\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}<0
,
x_{1},x_{10},x_{11},x_{19},x_{24}
and
x_{28}
should always be
0
.
(D_{h})
can be simplified as:

\displaystyle(D_{h1})=\max\hskip 8.50012pt

\displaystyle x_{29}

s.t.

\displaystyle-x_{2}+(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})x_{7}-\mu_{2}(x_{8}+x_{12}+x_{25})+(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{2})x_{15}+\left[\mu_{1}(1-\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{4}})-\mu_{2}\right])x_{20}=0

(B.12)

\displaystyle-x_{3}+(\mu_{2}-\mu_{3})(x_{15}+x_{20}+x_{25})-\mu_{3}(x_{13}+x_{16}+x_{26})+\left[\mu_{2}(1-\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{5}})-\mu_{3}\right]x_{21}=0

(B.13)

\displaystyle-x_{4}+(\mu_{3}-\mu_{4})(x_{8}+x_{16}+x_{21}+x_{25}+x_{26})-\mu_{4}(x_{9}+x_{14}+x_{17}+x_{27})+(\mu_{2}-\mu_{4})x_{12}

(B.14)

\displaystyle+\left[\mu_{3}(1-\frac{\mu_{5}}{\mu_{6}})-\mu_{4}\right]x_{22}=0

(B.15)

\displaystyle-x_{5}+(\mu_{3}-\mu_{5})x_{13}+(\mu_{4}-\mu_{5})(x_{17}+x_{22}+x_{26}+x_{27})-\mu_{5}x_{18}+\left[\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})-\mu_{5}\right]x_{23}=0

(B.16)

\displaystyle-x_{6}+(\mu_{4}-\mu_{6})x_{14}+(\mu_{5}-\mu_{6})(x_{9}+x_{18}+x_{23}+x_{27})-\mu_{6}x_{7}=0

(B.17)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{29}x_{i}=1

(B.18)

\displaystyle x_{1},x_{2},...,x_{29}\geq 0

(B.19)

We divide our analysis into 4 cases. The universe

\displaystyle U

\displaystyle=\{\mu_{1}\leq\mu_{2}\}\cup\{(\mu_{4}\leq\mu_{6})\cap(\mu_{5}\leq\mu_{6})\}\cup\{(\mu_{1}>\mu_{2})\cap((\mu_{4}>\mu_{6})\cup(\mu_{5}>\mu_{6}))\}

\displaystyle=\{\mu_{1}\leq\mu_{2}\}\cup\{(\mu_{4}\leq\mu_{6})\cap(\mu_{5}\leq\mu_{6})\}\cup\{(\mu_{1}>\mu_{2})\cap((\mu_{1}<\mu_{3})\cup(\mu_{2}<\mu_{3}))\}

\displaystyle=\{\mu_{1}\leq\mu_{2}\}\cup\{(\mu_{4}\leq\mu_{6})\cap(\mu_{5}\leq\mu_{6})\}\cup\{(\mu_{1}>\mu_{2})\cap(\mu_{3}>\mu_{2})\}

\displaystyle=\{\mu_{1}\leq\mu_{2}\}\cup\{(\mu_{4}\leq\mu_{6})\cap(\mu_{5}\leq\mu_{6})\}\cup\{(\mu_{1}>\mu_{2})\cap(\mu_{3}>\mu_{2})\cap(\mu_{3}\leq\mu_{4})\cap(\mu_{2}\leq\mu_{4})\}

\displaystyle\;\;\;\;\;\cup\{(\mu_{1}>\mu_{2})\cap(\mu_{3}>\mu_{2})\cap((\mu_{2}>\mu_{4})\cup(\mu_{3}>\mu 4))\}

\displaystyle=\{\mu_{1}\leq\mu_{2}\}\cup\{(\mu_{4}\leq\mu_{6})\cap(\mu_{5}\leq\mu_{6})\}\cup\{(\mu_{1}>\mu_{2})\cap(\mu_{4}\geq\mu_{3}>\mu_{2})\}\cup\{(\mu_{1}>\mu_{2})\cap(\mu_{3}>\mu_{2})\cap(\mu_{3}>\mu_{4})\}.

Case 1:

\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}\leq 0
.

From constraint (
B.12
), since
\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}\leq 0
,
\alpha_{1}-\mu_{2}<0
and
\mu_{1}(1-\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{4}})-\mu_{2}<0
, we have
x_{2}=x_{7}=x_{8}=x_{12}=x_{25}=x_{15}=x_{20}=0
.
(D_{h1})
can be further simplified as:

\displaystyle(D_{h2})=\max\hskip 8.50012pt

\displaystyle x_{29}

s.t.

\displaystyle-x_{3}-\mu_{3}(x_{13}+x_{16}+x_{26})+\left[\mu_{2}(1-\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{5}})-\mu_{3}\right]x_{21}=0

(B.20)

\displaystyle-x_{4}+(\mu_{3}-\mu_{4})(x_{16}+x_{21}+x_{26})-\mu_{4}(x_{9}+x_{14}+x_{17}+x_{27})+\left[\mu_{3}(1-\frac{\mu_{5}}{\mu_{6}})-\mu_{4}\right]x_{22}=0

(B.21)

\displaystyle-x_{5}+(\mu_{3}-\mu_{5})x_{13}+(\mu_{4}-\mu_{5})(x_{17}+x_{22}+x_{26}+x_{27})-\mu_{5}x_{18}+\left[\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})-\mu_{5}\right]x_{23}=0

(B.22)

\displaystyle-x_{6}+(\mu_{4}-\mu_{6})x_{14}+(\mu_{5}-\mu_{6})(x_{9}+x_{18}+x_{23}+x_{27})=0

(B.23)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{29}x_{i}=1

(B.24)

\displaystyle x_{1},x_{2},...,x_{29}\geq 0

(B.25)

Similarly, from constraint (
B.20
), since
\mu_{2}(1-\displaystyle\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{5}})-\mu_{3}<0
, we have
x_{3}=x_{13}=x_{16}=x_{26}=x_{21}=0
. Repeat the above steps, from constraint (
B.21
), we have
x_{4}=x_{9}=x_{14}=x_{17}=x_{27}=x_{22}=0
; from constraint (
B.22
), we have
x_{5}=x_{18}=x_{23}=0
; from constraint (
B.23
), we have
x_{6}=0
. So far, we have claimed that
x_{1}=x_{2}=...=x_{28}=0
, thus,
x_{29}=1
, which is the optimal solution of
(D_{h})
.

Case 2:

(\mu_{4}\leq\mu_{6})\cap(\mu_{5}\leq\mu_{6})
.

From constraint (
B.17
) of
(D_{h1})
, since
\mu_{4}-\mu_{6}\leq 0
and
\mu_{5}-\mu_{6}\leq 0
, we have
x_{7}=0
. Once we have
x_{7}=0
, we follow the same argument as in Case 1. Thus,
x_{1}=x_{2}=...=x_{28}=0
,
x_{29}=1
is the optimal solution of
(D_{h})
under Case 2.

Before we analyze Case 3 and Case 4 separately, we find that Case 3 and Case 4 both satisfy
\mu_{2}-\mu_{3}<0
and
\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}>0
. Thus, from constraint (
B.13
), since
\mu_{2}-\mu_{3}<0
and
\mu_{2}(1-\displaystyle\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{5}})-\mu_{3}<0
, we have
x_{3}=x_{15}=x_{20}=x_{25}=x_{13}=x_{16}=x_{26}=x_{21}=0
. Also, from constraint (
B.16
) in
(D_{h1})
, since
\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}>0
implies
\mu_{4}-\mu_{5}<0
, along with
\mu_{4}(1-\displaystyle\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})-\mu_{5}<0
, the only coefficient that could be positive is
(\mu_{3}-\mu_{5})
. However,
(\mu_{3}-\mu_{5})
is the coefficient of
x_{13}
and we already know that
x_{13}=0
. Thus, we have
x_{5}=x_{17}=x_{22}=x_{26}=x_{27}=x_{18}=x_{23}=0
.
(D_{h1})
can be simplified as:

\displaystyle(D_{h3})=\max\quad

\displaystyle x_{29}

s.t.

\displaystyle-x_{2}+(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})x_{7}-\mu_{2}(x_{8}+x_{12})=0

(B.26)

\displaystyle-x_{4}+(\mu_{3}-\mu_{4})x_{8}-\mu_{4}(x_{9}+x_{14})+(\mu_{2}-\mu_{4})x_{12}=0

(B.27)

\displaystyle-x_{6}+(\mu_{4}-\mu_{6})x_{14}+(\mu_{5}-\mu_{6})x_{9}-\mu_{6}x_{7}=0

(B.28)

\displaystyle x_{2}+x_{4}+x_{6}+x_{7}+x_{8}+x_{9}+x_{12}+x_{14}=1

(B.29)

\displaystyle x_{2},x_{4}...,x_{29}\geq 0

(B.30)

Case 3:

(\mu_{1}>\mu_{2})\cap(\mu_{4}\geq\mu_{3}>\mu_{2})
.

From constraint (
B.27
) in
(D_{h3})
, since
\mu_{3}-\mu_{4}\leq 0
and
\mu_{2}-\mu_{4}<0
, we have
x_{4}=x_{9}=x_{14}=0
. Note that we cannot decide if
x_{8}=x_{12}=0
here since

\mu_{3}-\mu_{4}
could be
0
. Then, from constraint (
B.28
) in
(D_{h3})
, since
x_{14}=x_{9}=0
, we have
x_{6}=x_{7}=0
. Consequently, from constraint (
B.26
) in
(D_{h3})
, since
x_{7}=0
, we have
x_{2}=x_{8}=x_{12}=0
. Thus,
x_{1}=x_{2}=...=x_{28}=0
,
x_{29}=1
is the optimal solution of
(D_{h})
under Case 3.

Case 4:

(\mu_{1}>\mu_{2})\cap(\mu_{3}>\mu_{2})\cap(\mu_{3}>\mu_{4})
.

In
(D_{h3})
, we see
x_{2},x_{4}
and
x_{6}
as slackness variables, and constraints (
B.26
) - (
B.28
) can be rewritten as:

\begin{split}&\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})x_{7}\geq\mu_{2}(x_{8}+x_{12})\\
(\mu_{3}-\mu_{4})x_{8}\geq\mu_{4}(x_{9}+x_{14})+(\mu_{4}-\mu_{2})x_{12}\\
(\mu_{5}-\mu_{6})x_{9}\geq\mu_{6}x_{7}+(\mu_{6}-\mu_{4})x_{14}\end{array}\right.\\
\Leftrightarrow&\left\{\begin{array}[]{lr}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})x_{7}\geq\mu_{2}(x_{8}+x_{12})\\
(\mu_{3}-\mu_{4})(x_{8}+x_{12})\geq\mu_{4}(x_{9}+x_{14})+(\mu_{3}-\mu_{2})x_{12}\\
(\mu_{5}-\mu_{6})(x_{9}+x_{14})\geq\mu_{6}x_{7}+(\mu_{5}-\mu_{4})x_{14}\end{array}\right.\\
\Rightarrow&\;(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})x_{7}\geq\mu_{2}(x_{8}+x_{12})\\
\Rightarrow&\;(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})x_{7}\geq\frac{\mu_{2}\mu_{4}}{\mu_{3}-\mu_{4}}(x_{9}+x_{14})+\frac{\mu_{2}(\mu_{3}-\mu_{2})}{\mu_{3}-\mu_{4}}x_{12}\\
\Rightarrow&\;(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})x_{7}\geq\frac{\mu_{2}\mu_{4}}{\mu_{3}-\mu_{4}}\left[\frac{\mu_{6}}{\mu_{5}-\mu_{6}}x_{7}+\frac{\mu_{5}-\mu_{4}}{\mu_{5}-\mu_{6}}x_{14}\right]+\frac{\mu_{2}(\mu_{3}-\mu_{2})}{\mu_{3}-\mu_{4}}x_{12}\\
\Leftrightarrow&\;(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})(\mu_{3}-\mu_{4})(\mu_{5}-\mu_{6})x_{7}\geq\mu_{2}\mu_{4}\mu_{6}x_{7}+\mu_{2}\mu_{4}(\mu_{5}-\mu_{4})x_{14}+\mu_{2}(\mu_{3}-\mu_{2})(\mu_{5}-\mu_{6})x_{12}\\
\Leftrightarrow&\;\left[(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})(\mu_{3}-\mu_{4})(\mu_{5}-\mu_{6})-\mu_{2}\mu_{4}\mu_{6}\right]x_{7}\geq\mu_{2}\mu_{4}(\mu_{5}-\mu_{4})x_{14}+\mu_{2}(\mu_{3}-\mu_{2})(\mu_{5}-\mu_{6})x_{12}\end{split}

Note that
(\mu_{1}>\mu_{2})\cap(\mu_{3}>\mu_{2})\cap(\mu_{3}>\mu_{4})
implies
(\mu_{5}>\mu_{4})\cap(\mu_{5}>\mu_{6})\cap(\mu_{1}>\mu_{6})
. So we have
\mu_{5}-\mu_{4}>0
,
\mu_{3}-\mu_{2}>0
and
\mu_{5}-\mu_{6}>0
. Thus, the right hand side of the above inequality is larger than or equal to
0
. As Chen and Ye proved in
[
6
]
, under
m_{2}+m_{4}+m_{6}<2
, we have
\left[(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})(\mu_{3}-\mu_{4})(\mu_{5}-\mu_{6})-\mu_{2}\mu_{4}\mu_{6}\right]<0
since this is equivalent to
m_{1}m_{3}m_{5}-(m_{2}-m_{1})(m_{4}-m_{3})(m_{6}-m_{5})>0
and we can verify that
m_{1}m_{3}m_{5}-(m_{2}-m_{1})(m_{4}-m_{3})(m_{6}-m_{5})=(m_{1}+m_{3}-m_{2})\left[(m_{2}-m_{1})m_{6}+m_{1}m_{5}\right]=(2-m_{4}-m_{6}-m_{2})\left[(m_{2}-m_{1})m_{6}+m_{1}m_{5}\right]>0
.

Consequently, under
m_{2}+m_{4}+m_{6}<2
, we have
x_{7}=x_{14}=x_{12}=0
. From constraint (
B.26
), since
x_{7}=0
,we have
x_{2}=x_{8}=0
. From constraint (
B.27
), since
x_{8}=x_{12}=0
, we have
x_{4}=x_{9}=0
. From constraint (
B.28
), since
x_{14}=x_{9}=0
, we have
x_{6}=0
. So far, we have argued that under
m_{2}+m_{4}+m_{6}<2
,
x_{1}=x_{2}=...=x_{28}=0
, thus
x_{29}=1
, and this is the optimal solution of
(D_{h})
under Case 4 when
m_{2}+m_{4}+m_{6}<2
.

To sum up, if we have
m_{2}+m_{4}+m_{6}<2
, then the optimal value of
(D_{h})
is
1
, which means that there exists a vector
h
that satisfies all the 23 inequalities we derived for a balanced DHV network. According to Theorem
7
, we can conclude that under
m_{2}+m_{4}+m_{6}<2
,
\alpha_{1}\in(\max\{\frac{m_{6}-m_{5}}{m_{2}m_{6}},\frac{m_{5}-m_{4}}{m_{1}m_{5}},\frac{m_{6}-m_{4}}{m_{1}m_{6}},0\},1)
, a balanced DHV network satisfies SSC under any queue-ratio policies.

Appendix C
Detailed Proofs for Balanced Push Started Lu-Kumar Networks

C.1
Chen-
\mathcal{S}
in Balanced push started Lu-Kumar Networks

In this appendix, we prove that the reflection matrices for all the static-priority cases of a balanced push started Lu-Kumar network (
m_{1}+m_{3}+m_{4}=1
;
m_{2}+m_{5}=1
) with
\alpha_{1}<1
are Chen-
\mathcal{S}
, and invertible and have same-sign determinants if
m_{2}m_{4}>m_{3}m_{5}
. In turn, Theorem
4
allows us to say that the refection matrix is invertible and Chen-
\mathcal{S}
for any ratio matrix
\Delta
of a balanced push started Lu-Kumar network if
m_{2}m_{4}>m_{3}m_{5}
.

For each of the 6 static priority policies, to prove that the corresponding
(R,\theta)
is Chen-
\mathcal{S}
, we must show that
R
is completely-
\mathcal{S}
, and consider all the 3 partitions of
\mathcal{J}
and show that there exists a positive vector
h\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
such that (
2.8
) satisfies for all the 3 partitions.

Proof.

Proof for lowest-priority 1, 2 (Chen-S).

In the balanced push started Lu-Kumar network with class 1 having the lowest priority in station 1 and class 2 having the lowest priority in station 2, we have
J=2
,
K=5
,
\rho=\left[\alpha_{1}\ \alpha_{1}\right]^{\prime},

R=\left[\begin{matrix}1&m_{1}-1\mcr-\displaystyle\frac{m_{2}}{m_{1}}&\displaystyle\frac{m_{2}}{m_{1}}\end{matrix}\right],

\displaystyle\theta=R(\rho-e)=(\alpha_{1}-1)\left[\begin{matrix}m_{1}\mcr 0\end{matrix}\right].

It is clear that the reflection matrix
R
is completely-
\mathcal{S}
, since
R
is
2\times 2
,
R^{-1}=CMQ\Delta>0
and all its diagonal elements are positive.

Partition 1:
a=1;b=2

\theta_{b}=\theta_{2}=0,\;R_{b}^{-1}=R_{2}^{-1}=\frac{m_{1}}{m_{2}}\Rightarrow u=-R_{b}^{-1}\theta_{b}=0\Rightarrow R_{ab}u=0.

Thus,
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-1)m_{1}
. Since
\alpha_{1}<1
, taking
h_{1}>0
, we ensure
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]<0
.

Partition 2:
a=2;b=1

\theta_{b}=\theta_{1}=(\alpha_{1}-1)m_{1},\;R_{b}^{-1}=R_{1}^{-1}=1\Rightarrow u=-R_{b}^{-1}\theta_{b}=(1-\alpha_{1})m_{1};

R_{ab}=-\frac{m_{2}}{m_{1}}\Rightarrow R_{ab}u=(\alpha_{1}-1)m_{2}.

Thus,
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]=h_{2}(\alpha_{1}-1)m_{2}
. Since
\alpha_{1}<1
, taking
h_{2}>0
, we ensure
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]<0
.

Partition 3:
a=1,2;b=\varnothing

h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]=h^{\prime}\theta=(\alpha_{1}-1)h_{1}m_{1}

Since
\alpha_{1}<1
, taking
h_{1}>0
, we ensure
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]<0
.

We have shown, then, that exists a positive vector
h\in{R^{2}}
as required so that
(R,\theta)
under the static-priority policy 1, 2 is Chen-
\mathcal{S}
.
∎

∎

Proof.

Proof for lowest-priority 1, 5 (Chen-S).

In the balanced push started Lu-Kumar network with class 1 having the lowest priority in station 1 and class 5 having the lowest priority in station 2, we have
J=2,K=5,\rho=\left[\alpha_{1}\ \alpha_{1}\right]^{\prime},

R=\left[\begin{matrix}m_{1}&0\mcr-1&1\end{matrix}\right],

\displaystyle\theta=R(\rho-e)=(\alpha_{1}-1)\left[\begin{matrix}m_{1}\mcr 0\end{matrix}\right].

It is clear that the reflection matrix
R
is completely-
\mathcal{S}
, since
R
is
2\times 2
,
R^{-1}=CMQ\Delta>0
and all its diagonal elements are positive.

Partition 1:
a=1;b=2

\theta_{b}=\theta_{2}=0,\;R_{b}^{-1}=R_{2}^{-1}=1\Rightarrow u=-R_{b}^{-1}\theta_{b}=0\Rightarrow R_{ab}u=0.

Thus,
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-1)m_{1}
. Since
\alpha_{1}<1
, taking
h_{1}>0
, we ensure
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]<0
.

Partition 2:
a=2;b=1

\theta_{b}=\theta_{1}=(\alpha_{1}-1)m_{1},\;R_{b}^{-1}=R_{1}^{-1}=\frac{1}{m_{1}}\Rightarrow u=-R_{b}^{-1}\theta_{b}=1-\alpha_{1};

R_{ab}=-1\Rightarrow R_{ab}u=\alpha_{1}-1.

Thus,
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]=h_{2}(\alpha_{1}-1)
. Since
\alpha_{1}<1
, taking
h_{2}>0
, we ensure
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]<0
.

Partition 3:
a=1,2;b=\varnothing

h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]=h^{\prime}\theta=(\alpha_{1}-1)h_{1}m_{1}

Since
\alpha_{1}<1
, taking
h_{1}>0
, we ensure
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]<0
.

We have shown, then, that exists a positive vector
h\in{R^{2}}
as required so that
(R,\theta)
under the static-priority policy 1, 5 is Chen-
\mathcal{S}
.
∎

∎

Proof.

Proof for lowest-priority 3,2 (Chen-S).

In the balanced push started Lu-Kumar network with class 3 having the lowest priority in station 1 and class 2 having the lowest priority in station 2, we have
J=2,K=5,\rho=\left[\alpha_{1}\ \alpha_{1}\right]^{\prime},

R=\left[\begin{matrix}\displaystyle\frac{m_{3}}{m_{2}(1-m_{1})}&-\displaystyle\frac{m_{3}}{m_{2}}\mcr-\displaystyle\frac{m_{5}}{1-m_{1}}&1\end{matrix}\right],

\displaystyle\theta=R(\rho-e)=(\alpha_{1}-1)\left[\begin{matrix}\displaystyle\frac{m_{3}m_{1}}{m_{2}(1-m_{1})}\mcr-\displaystyle\frac{m_{2}-m_{1}}{1-m_{1}}\end{matrix}\right].

It is clear that the reflection matrix
R
is completely-
\mathcal{S}
, since
R
is
2\times 2
,
R^{-1}=CMQ\Delta>0
and all its diagonal elements are positive.

Partition 1:
a=1;b=2

\theta_{b}=\theta_{2}=(\alpha_{1}-1)\frac{m_{2}-m_{1}}{1-m_{1}},\;R_{b}^{-1}=R_{2}^{-1}=1\Rightarrow u=-R_{b}^{-1}\theta_{b}=(1-\alpha_{1})\frac{m_{2}-m_{1}}{1-m_{1}};

R_{ab}=-\frac{m_{3}}{m_{2}}\Rightarrow R_{ab}u=(\alpha_{1}-1)\frac{m_{3}(m_{2}-m_{1})}{m_{2}(1-m_{1})}.

Thus,
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-1)\displaystyle\frac{m_{1}m_{3}+m_{3}(m_{2}-m_{1})}{m_{2}(1-m_{1})}=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-1)\frac{m_{3}}{1-m_{1}}
. Since
\alpha_{1}<1
, taking
h_{1}>0
, we ensure
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]<0
.

Partition 2:
a=2;b=1

\theta_{b}=\theta_{1}=(\alpha_{1}-1)\frac{m_{3}m_{1}}{m_{2}(1-m_{1})},\;R_{b}^{-1}=R_{1}^{-1}=\frac{m_{2}(1-m_{1})}{m_{3}}\Rightarrow u=-R_{b}^{-1}\theta_{b}=(1-\alpha_{1})m_{1};

R_{ab}=-\frac{m_{5}}{1-m_{1}}\Rightarrow R_{ab}u=(\alpha_{1}-1)\frac{m_{1}m_{5}}{1-m_{1}}.

Thus,
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]=h_{2}(\alpha_{1}-1)\displaystyle\frac{m_{2}-m_{1}+m_{1}m_{5}}{1-m_{1}}=h_{2}(\alpha_{1}-1)\frac{m_{2}-m_{1}(1-m_{5})}{1-m_{1}}=h_{2}(\alpha_{1}-1)\frac{m_{2}-m_{1}m_{2}}{1-m_{1}}=(\alpha_{1}-1)h_{2}m_{2}
. Since
\alpha_{1}<1
, taking
h_{2}>0
, we ensure
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]<0
.

Partition 3:
a=1,2;b=\varnothing

h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]=h^{\prime}\theta=(\alpha_{1}-1)\left[\frac{m_{3}m_{1}}{m_{2}(1-m_{1})}h_{1}+\frac{m_{2}-m_{1}}{1-m_{1}}h_{2}\right]

Since
\alpha_{1}<1
,
\displaystyle\frac{m_{3}m_{1}}{m_{2}(1-m_{1})}>0
, fixing
h_{1}>0
large enough, we ensure
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]<0
.

We have shown, then, that exists a positive vector
h\in{R^{2}}
with
h_{1}
being large enough as required so that
(R,\theta)
under the static-priority policy 3, 2 is Chen-
\mathcal{S}
.
∎

Proof.

Proof for lowest-priority 3, 5 (Chen-S).

In the balanced push started Lu-Kumar network with class 3 having lowest priority in station 1 and class 5 having lowest priority in station 2, we have
J=2
,
K=5
,
\rho=\left[\alpha_{1}\ \alpha_{1}\right]^{\prime},

R=\left[\begin{matrix}\displaystyle\frac{m_{3}}{1-m_{1}}&0\mcr-\displaystyle\frac{m_{5}}{1-m_{1}}&1\end{matrix}\right],

\displaystyle\theta=R(\rho-e)=\frac{\alpha_{1}-1}{1-m_{1}}\left[\begin{matrix}m_{3}\mcr m_{2}-m_{1}\end{matrix}\right].

It is clear that the reflection matrix
R
is completely-
\mathcal{S}
, since
R
is
2\times 2
,
R^{-1}=CMQ\Delta>0
and all its diagonal elements are positive.

Partition 1:
a=1;b=2

\theta_{b}=\theta_{2}=(\alpha_{1}-1)\frac{m_{2}-m_{1}}{1-m_{1}},\;R_{b}^{-1}=R_{2}^{-1}=1\Rightarrow u=-R_{b}^{-1}\theta_{b}=(\alpha_{1}-1)\frac{m_{1}-m_{2}}{1-m_{1}};

R_{ab}=0\Rightarrow R_{ab}u=0.

Thus,
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-1)\displaystyle\frac{m_{3}}{1-m_{1}}
. Since
\alpha_{1}<1
, taking
h_{1}>0
, we ensure
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]<0
.

Partition 2:
a=2;b=1

\theta_{b}=\theta_{1}=(\alpha_{1}-1)\frac{m_{3}}{1-m_{1}},\;R_{b}^{-1}=R_{1}^{-1}=\frac{1-m_{1}}{m_{3}}\Rightarrow u=-R_{b}^{-1}\theta_{b}=1-\alpha_{1};

R_{ab}=-\frac{m_{5}}{1-m_{1}}\Rightarrow R_{ab}u=(\alpha_{1}-1)\frac{m_{5}}{1-m_{1}}.

Thus,
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]=h_{2}(\alpha_{1}-1)\displaystyle\frac{m_{2}-m_{1}+m_{5}}{1-m_{1}}=(\alpha_{1}-1)h_{2}
. Since
\alpha_{1}<1
, taking
h_{2}>0
, we ensure
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]<0
.

Partition 3:
a=1,2;b=\varnothing

h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]=h^{\prime}\theta=\frac{\alpha_{1}-1}{1-m_{1}}\left[m_{3}h_{1}+(m_{2}-m_{1})h_{2}\right]

Since
\alpha_{1}<1
,
1-m_{1}>0
, fixing
h_{1}>0
large enough, we ensure
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]<0
.

We have shown, then, that exists a positive vector
h\in{R^{2}}
with
h_{1}
being large enough as required so that
(R,\theta)
under the static-priority policy 3, 5 is Chen-
\mathcal{S}
.
