∎

Proof.

Proof for lowest-priority 4, 2 (Chen-S).

In the balanced push started Lu-Kumar network with class 4 having lowest priority in station 1 and class 2 having lowest priority in station 2, we have
J=2
,
K=5
,
\rho=\left[\alpha_{1}\ \alpha_{1}\right]^{\prime},

R=\left[\begin{matrix}\displaystyle\frac{m_{4}}{m_{2}m_{4}-m_{3}m_{5}}&-\displaystyle\frac{m_{4}(1-m_{1})}{m_{2}m_{4}-m_{3}m_{5}}\mcr-\displaystyle\frac{m_{2}m_{5}}{m_{2}m_{4}-m_{3}m_{5}}&\displaystyle\frac{m_{2}m_{4}}{m_{2}m_{4}-m_{3}m_{5}}\end{matrix}\right],

\displaystyle\theta=R(\rho-e)=\frac{\alpha_{1}-1}{m_{2}m_{4}-m_{3}m_{5}}\left[\begin{matrix}m_{1}m_{4}\mcr m_{2}(m_{4}-m_{5})\end{matrix}\right].

It is clear that the reflection matrix
R
is completely-
\mathcal{S}
if
m_{2}m_{4}>m_{3}m_{5}
, since
R
is
2\times 2
,
R^{-1}=CMQ\Delta>0
and all its diagonal elements are positive when
m_{2}m_{4}>m_{3}m_{5}
.

Partition 1:
a=1;b=2

\theta_{b}=\theta_{2}=\frac{\alpha_{1}-1}{m_{2}m_{4}-m_{3}m_{5}}m_{2}(m_{4}-m_{5}),\;R_{b}^{-1}=R_{2}^{-1}=\frac{m_{2}m_{4}-m_{3}m_{5}}{m_{2}m_{4}}\Rightarrow u=-R_{b}^{-1}\theta_{b}=(1-\alpha_{1})\frac{m_{4}-m_{5}}{m_{4}};

R_{ab}=-\frac{m_{4}(1-m_{1})}{m_{2}m_{4}-m_{3}m_{5}}\Rightarrow R_{ab}u=\frac{\alpha_{1}-1}{m_{2}m_{4}-m_{3}m_{5}}(m_{4}-m_{5})(1-m_{1}).

Thus,
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]=\displaystyle\frac{\alpha_{1}-1}{m_{2}m_{4}-m_{3}m_{5}}h_{1}(m_{4}-m_{5})(1-m_{1})(m_{1}m_{4}+m_{4}-m_{1}m_{4}-m_{5}+m_{1}m_{5})=\frac{\alpha_{1}-1}{m_{2}m_{4}-m_{3}m_{5}}h_{1}(m_{4}-m_{5}+m_{1}m_{5})
. Since
m_{4}-m_{5}+m_{1}m_{5}=m_{4}-m_{5}(1-m_{1})=m_{4}-m_{5}(m_{3}+m_{4})=m_{4}(1-m_{5})-m_{3}m_{5}=m_{4}m_{2}-m_{3}m_{5}
,
\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]=(\alpha_{1}-1)h_{1}
. As
\alpha_{1}<1
, taking
h_{1}>0
, we ensure
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]<0
.

Partition 2:
a=2;b=1

\theta_{b}=\theta_{1}=\frac{\alpha_{1}-1}{m_{2}m_{4}-m_{3}m_{5}}m_{1}m_{4},\;R_{b}^{-1}=R_{1}^{-1}=\frac{m_{2}m_{4}-m_{3}m_{5}}{m_{4}}\Rightarrow u=-R_{b}^{-1}\theta_{b}=(1-\alpha_{1})m_{1};

R_{ab}=-\frac{m_{2}m_{5}}{m_{2}m_{4}-m_{3}m_{5}}\Rightarrow R_{ab}u=(\alpha_{1}-1)\frac{m_{1}m_{2}m_{5}}{m_{2}m_{4}-m_{3}m_{5}}.

Thus,
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]=\displaystyle\frac{\alpha_{1}-1}{m_{2}m_{4}-m_{3}m_{5}}h_{2}\left[m_{2}(m_{4}-m_{5})+m_{1}m_{2}m_{5}\right]
. Since
m_{2}(m_{4}-m_{5})+m_{1}m_{2}m_{5}=m_{2}m_{4}+m_{2}m_{5}(m_{1}-1)=m_{2}m_{4}+m_{2}m_{5}(-m_{3}-m_{4})=m_{2}m_{5}(1-m_{5})-m_{2}m_{3}m_{5}=m_{2}^{2}m_{4}-m_{2}m_{3}m_{5}=m_{2}(m_{2}m_{4}-m_{3}m_{5})
, we have
\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]=(\alpha_{1}-1)h_{2}m_{2}
. As
\alpha_{1}<1
, taking
h_{2}>0
, we ensure
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]<0
.

Partition 3:
a=1,2;b=\varnothing

h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]=h^{\prime}\theta=\frac{\alpha_{1}-1}{m_{2}m_{4}-m_{3}m_{5}}\left[m_{1}m_{4}h_{1}+m_{2}(m_{4}-m_{5})h_{2}\right]

Since
\alpha_{1}<1
,
m_{1}m_{4}>0
, and we are not sure the sigh of
m_{2}(m_{4}-m_{5})
, if
m_{2}m_{4}>m_{3}m_{5}
is satisfied, fixing
h_{1}>0
large enough, and
h_{2}>0
, we ensure
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]<0
.

We have shown, if
m_{2}m_{4}>m_{3}m_{5}
, then, that exists a positive vector
h\in{R^{2}}
with
h_{1}
being large enough as required so that
(R,\theta)
under the static-priority policy 4, 2 is Chen-
\mathcal{S}
.
∎

∎

Proof.

Proof for lowest-priority 4, 5 (Chen-S).

In the balanced push started Lu-Kumar network with class 4 having lowest priority in station 1 and class 5 having lowest priority in station 2, we have
J=2
,
K=5
,
\rho=\left[\alpha_{1}\ \alpha_{1}\right]^{\prime},

R=\left[\begin{matrix}1&0\mcr-\displaystyle\frac{m_{5}}{m_{4}}&1\end{matrix}\right],

\displaystyle\theta=R(\rho-e)=(\alpha_{1}-1)\left[\begin{matrix}1\mcr\displaystyle\frac{m_{4}-m_{5}}{m_{4}}\end{matrix}\right].

It is clear that the reflection matrix
R
is completely-
\mathcal{S}
, since
R
is
2\times 2
,
R^{-1}=CMQ\Delta>0
and all its diagonal elements are positive.

Partition 1:
a=1;b=2

\theta_{b}=\theta_{2}=(\alpha_{1}-1)\frac{m_{4}-m_{5}}{m_{4}},\;R_{b}^{-1}=R_{2}^{-1}=1\Rightarrow u=-R_{b}^{-1}\theta_{b}=(1-\alpha_{1})\frac{m_{4}-m_{5}}{m_{4}};

R_{ab}=0\Rightarrow R_{ab}u=0.

Thus,
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-1)
. Since
\alpha_{1}<1
, taking
h_{1}>0
, we ensure
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]<0
.

Partition 2:
a=2;b=1

\theta_{b}=\theta_{1}=\alpha_{1}-1,\;R_{b}^{-1}=R_{1}^{-1}=1\Rightarrow u=-R_{b}^{-1}\theta_{b}=1-\alpha_{1};

R_{ab}=-\frac{m_{5}}{m_{4}}\Rightarrow R_{ab}u=(\alpha_{1}-1)\frac{m_{5}}{m_{4}}.

Thus,
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]=(\alpha_{1}-1)h_{2}\displaystyle\frac{m_{4}-m_{5}+m_{5}}{m_{4}}=(\alpha_{1}-1)h_{2}
. Since
\alpha_{1}<1
, taking
h_{2}>0
, we ensure
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]<0
.

Partition 3:
a=1,2;b=\varnothing

h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]=h^{\prime}\theta=(\alpha_{1}-1)(h_{1}+\frac{m_{4}-m_{5}}{m_{4}}h_{2})

Since
\alpha_{1}<1
, fixing
h_{1}>0
large enough, we ensure
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]<0
.

We have shown, then, that exists a positive vector
h\in{R^{2}}
with
h_{1}
being large enough as required so that
(R,\theta)
under the static-priority policy 4, 5 is Chen-
\mathcal{S}
.
∎∎

Now, we complete the proof that the 6 static-priority cases are all Chen-
\mathcal{S}
if
m_{2}m_{4}>m_{3}m_{5}
.

The reflection matrices for the 6 balanced push started Lu-Kumar networks following static-priority policies are all invertible. Now we check the sign of determinants of them. We can calculate that
det(R_{1,2})=m_{2}>0
,
det(R_{1,5})=m_{1}>0
,
det(R_{3,2})=\displaystyle\frac{m_{3}}{1-m_{1}}>0
,
det(R_{3,5})=\displaystyle\frac{m_{3}}{1-m_{1}}>0
,
det(R_{4,2})=\displaystyle\frac{m_{2}m_{4}}{m_{2}m_{4}-m_{3}m_{5}}
,
det(R_{4,5})=1>0
, where the subscript label indicates the classes with the lowest priority in each station. If we want the sign of the determinants to be the same, we need
m_{2}m_{4}>m_{3}m_{5}
. Thus, we can conclude that the reflection matrix for a balanced push started Lu-Kumar network is invertible for any ratio matrix
\Delta
if
m_{2}m_{4}>m_{3}m_{5}
.

According to Theorem
4
, in a balanced push started Lu-Kumar network with
\alpha_{1}<1
, the reflection matrix is invertible and
(R,\theta)
is Chen-
\mathcal{S}
for any ratio matrix
\Delta
if
m_{2}m_{4}>m_{3}m_{5}
.

C.2
The SSC Inequalities for Balanced Push Started Lu-Kumar Networks

Similarly as the analysis in Appendix
B.2
, we get the SSC conditions for balanced push started Lu-Kumar networks in this appendix. Note that in balanced push started Lu-Kumar networks, we have
m_{1}+m_{3}+m_{4}=1
and
m_{2}+m_{5}=1
.
We check the 6 static priority policies of balanced push started Lu-Kumar networks for (
B.1
) in the following analysis. As mentioned in §
4.2
, we use a fixed priority order as
1>3>4
(in station 1) within the set
\{\ell\in\mathcal{C}(j):Z_{l}(t)-\delta_{l}W_{j}(t)>0\}
as the “tie-breaking” rule.

Same as Appendix
B.2
, given a policy
\pi
, let
\dot{Y}^{\pi}(t)=\sum_{k\in\mathcal{H}_{\pi}:z_{k}>0}h_{k}\dot{\overline{Z}_{k}^{\pi}}(t)
. Then, we want
\dot{Y}^{\pi}(t)<0
with a common vector
h\in\mathbb{R}^{K}_{++}
for any regular time
t
with
\lVert\overline{Z}^{\pi}_{\mathcal{H}_{\pi}}(t)\rVert>0
, i.e., at least one of the high-priority queues is not empty, under all the 6 static priority policies. We omit
\pi
when it is fixed. In balanced push started Lu-Kumar networks,
m_{k}<1
and thus
\mu_{k}>1
for all
k\in\mathcal{K}
. Denote
I_{k}(t)
as the input rate of class
k
at time
t
;
O_{k}(t)
as the output rate of class
k
at time
t
. Then,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{k}(t)=I_{k}(t)-O_{k}(t)\text{ for }k\in\mathcal{K};I_{k}(t)=O_{k-1}(t)\text{ for }k\in\mathcal{K}/\{1\};I_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}
. In this section, we use a set of lowest-priority classes to represent static priority policies, for example,
(1, 2) stands for a static priority policy that class 1 and 2 have the lowest priorities in each station.

Case 1:
(1, 2)

1.

\overline{Z}_{3}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{5}(t)>0.

It is clear that server 1 serves queue 3, server 2 serves queue 5, then
O_{3}(t)=\mu_{3}
,
O_{5}(t)=\mu_{5}
,
O_{1}(t)=O_{2}(t)=O_{4}(t)=0
. So
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=0-\mu_{3}=-\mu_{3}
,

\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=\mu_{3}-0=\mu_{3}
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=0-\mu_{5}=-\mu_{5}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)=-h_{3}\mu_{3}+h_{4}\mu_{3}-h_{5}\mu_{5}<0
.

2.

\overline{Z}_{3}(t)=0,\overline{Z}_{4}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{5}(t)>0.

It is clear that
O_{1}(t)=0
, server 2 serves queue 5, then
O_{5}(t)=\mu_{5}
,
O_{2}(t)=0
. Since
I_{3}(t)=O_{2}(t)=0
and
\overline{Z}_{3}(t)=0
, we have
O_{3}(t)=0
and serve 1 serves queue 4, which means
O_{4}(t)=\mu_{4}
. So
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=0-0=0
,

\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=0-\mu_{4}=-\mu_{4}
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=\mu_{4}-\mu_{5}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)=-h_{4}\mu_{4}+h_{5}(\mu_{4}-\mu_{5})<0
.

3.

\overline{Z}_{3}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{5}(t)=0.

It is clear that server 1 serves queue 3, then
O_{3}(t)=\mu_{3}
,
O_{1}(t)=O_{4}(t)=0
. Since
O_{4}(t)=0
and
\overline{Z}_{5}(t)=0
, we have
O_{5}(t)=0
.
So
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=\mu_{3}-0=\mu_{3}
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=0-0=0
.

If
\overline{Z}_{2}(t)=0
, since
O_{1}(t)=0
, we have
O_{2}(t)=0
, then
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=0-\mu_{3}=-\mu_{3}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)=-h_{3}\mu_{3}+h_{4}\mu_{3}<0
.

If
\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0
, since
O_{5}(t)=0
, server 2 serves queue 2, we have
O_{2}(t)=\mu_{2}
, then
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=\mu_{2}-\mu_{3}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{3}(\mu_{2}-\mu_{3})+h_{4}\mu_{3}<0
.

Note that
h_{3}(\mu_{2}-\mu_{3})+h_{4}\mu_{3}\geq-h_{3}\mu_{3}+h_{4}\mu_{3}
, then it is enough to only consider
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{3}(\mu_{2}-\mu_{3})+h_{4}\mu_{3}<0
in this situation.

4.

\overline{Z}_{3}(t)=0,\overline{Z}_{4}(t)=0,\overline{Z}_{5}(t)>0.

It is clear that server 2 serves queue 5, then
O_{5}(t)=\mu_{5}
,
O_{2}(t)=0
. Since
I_{3}(t)=O_{2}(t)=0
and
\overline{Z}_{3}(t)=0
, we have
O_{3}(t)=0
. Since
I_{4}(t)=O_{3}(t)=0
and
\overline{Z}_{4}(t)=0
, we have
O_{4}(t)=0
. So
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=0-0=0
,

\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=0-0=0
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=0-\mu_{5}=-\mu_{5}
. It obviously holds that
\dot{Y}(t)=-h_{5}\mu_{5}<0
as required.

5.

\overline{Z}_{3}(t)=0,\overline{Z}_{4}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{5}(t)=0.

It is clear that server 1 serves queue 4, then
O_{4}(t)=\mu_{4}
,
O_{1}(t)=O_{3}(t)=0
. So
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=0-\mu_{4}=-\mu_{4}
. If
\mu_{4}>\mu_{5}
,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=\mu_{4}-O_{5}(t)>0
, then
t
is not differentiable; if
\mu_{4}\leq\mu_{5}
,
O_{5}(t)=\mu_{5}
,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=\mu_{4}-\mu_{4}=0
,
\dot{\overline{T}}_{5}(t)=\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{5}}
. If
\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0
, we have
O_{2}(t)=\mu_{2}(1-\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{5}})
, and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=\mu_{2}(1-\frac{\mu_{4}}{\mu_{5}})-0>0
, then
t
is not differentiable; if
\overline{Z}_{2}(t)=0
, since
I_{2}(t)=O_{1}(t)=0
, we have
O_{2}(t)=0
, so
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=0-0=0
. It obviously holds that
\dot{Y}(t)=-h_{4}\mu_{4}<0
as required.

To sum up, except for the positive condition for common vector
h
, we need the following conditions from case 1:

\displaystyle-h_{3}\mu_{3}+h_{4}\mu_{3}-h_{5}\mu_{5}<0,

\displaystyle-h_{4}\mu_{4}+h_{5}(\mu_{4}-\mu_{5})<0,

\displaystyle h_{3}(\mu_{2}-\mu_{3})+h_{4}\mu_{3}<0.

Note that
-h_{3}\mu_{3}+h_{4}\mu_{3}-h_{5}\mu_{5}\leq h_{3}(\mu_{2}-\mu_{3})+h_{4}\mu_{3}
, thus, it is enough to consider only the following two conditions from case 1:

\displaystyle-h_{4}\mu_{4}+h_{5}(\mu_{4}-\mu_{5})<0,

\displaystyle h_{3}(\mu_{2}-\mu_{3})+h_{4}\mu_{3}<0.

Case 2:
(1, 5)

1.

\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{3}(t)>0.

It is clear that server 1 serves queue 3, server 2 serves queue 2, then
O_{3}(t)=\mu_{3}
,
O_{2}(t)=\mu_{2}
,
O_{1}(t)=O_{4}(t)=O_{5}(t)=0
. So
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=0-\mu_{2}=-\mu_{2}
,

\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=\mu_{2}-\mu_{3}
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=\mu_{3}-0=\mu_{3}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)=-h_{2}\mu_{2}+h_{3}(\mu_{2}-\mu_{3})+h_{4}\mu_{3}<0
.

2.

\overline{Z}_{2}(t)=0,\overline{Z}_{3}(t)>0

It is clear that server 1 serves queue 3, then
O_{3}(t)=\mu_{3}
,
O_{1}(t)=O_{4}(t)=0
. Since
I_{2}(t)=O_{1}(t)=0
and
\overline{Z}_{2}(t)=0
, we have
O_{2}(t)=0
.
So
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=0-0=0
,

\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=0-\mu_{3}=-\mu_{3}
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=\mu_{3}-0=\mu_{3}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)=-h_{3}\mu_{3}+h_{4}\mu_{3}<0
.

3.

\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{3}(t)=0,\overline{Z}_{4}(t)>0.

It is clear that server 1 serves queue 4, and server 2 serves queue 2, then
O_{4}(t)=\mu_{4}
,
O_{2}(t)=\mu_{2}
,
O_{1}(t)=O_{3}(t)=O_{5}(t)=0
. Thus,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=\mu_{2}-0=\mu_{2}>0
, which means that
t
is not differentiable.

4.

\overline{Z}_{2}(t)=0,\overline{Z}_{3}(t)=0,\overline{Z}_{4}(t)>0.

It is clear that server 1 serves queue 4, then
O_{4}(t)=\mu_{4}
,
O_{1}(t)=O_{3}(t)=0
. Since
I_{2}(t)=O_{1}(t)=0
and
\overline{Z}_{2}(t)=0
, we have
O_{2}(t)=0
. Since
I_{3}(t)=O_{2}(t)=0
and
\overline{Z}_{3}(t)=0
, we have
O_{3}(t)=0
. So
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=0-0=0
,

\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=0-0=0
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=0-\mu_{4}=-\mu_{4}
. It obviously holds that
\dot{Y}(t)=-h_{4}\mu_{4}<0
as required.

5.

\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{3}(t)=0,\overline{Z}_{4}(t)=0.

It is clear that server 2 serves queue 2, then
O_{2}(t)=\mu_{2}
,
O_{5}(t)=0
. If
\mu_{2}>\mu_{3}
, we have
O_{3}(t)=\mu_{3}
, and then
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=\mu_{2}-\mu_{3}>0
, then
t
is not differentiable; if
\mu_{2}\leq\mu_{3}
,
O_{3}(t)=\mu_{2}
,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=\mu_{2}-\mu_{2}=0
,
\dot{\overline{T}}_{3}(t)=\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{3}}
. If
\mu_{2}>\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{3}})
, we have
O_{4}(t)=\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{3}})
, and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=\mu_{2}-\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{3}})>0
, then
t
is not differentiable; if
\mu_{2}\leq\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{3}})
, then
O_{4}(t)=\mu_{2}
,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=\mu_{2}-\mu_{2}=0
and
\dot{\overline{T}}_{1}(t)=1-\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{3}}-\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{4}}
. So,
O_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}
or
O_{1}(t)=\mu_{1}(1-\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{3}}-\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{4}})
. Thus,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{2}<0
or
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=\mu_{1}(1-\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{3}}-\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{4}})-\mu_{2}
. We have
\mu_{1}(1-\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{3}}-\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{4}})-\mu_{2}<0
since
\mu_{1}(1-\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{3}}-\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{4}})-\mu_{2}<0\Leftrightarrow\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{1}}+\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{3}}+\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{4}}>1\Leftrightarrow\frac{m_{1}+m_{3}+m_{4}}{m_{2}}>1\Leftrightarrow\frac{1}{m_{2}}>1
, which is true obviously. So, we have
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)<0
,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=0
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=0
. Thus,
\dot{Y}(t)<0
is satisfied obviously, as required.

To sum up, except for the positive condition for common vector
h
, we need the following conditions from case 2:

\displaystyle-h_{2}\mu_{2}+h_{3}(\mu_{2}-\mu_{3})+h_{4}\mu_{3}<0

\displaystyle-h_{3}\mu_{3}+h_{4}\mu_{3}<0.

Case 3:
(3, 2)

1.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{5}(t)>0.

It is clear that
I_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}
, server 1 serves queue 1, server 2 serves queue 5, then
O_{1}(t)=\mu_{1}
,
O_{5}(t)=\mu_{5}
,
O_{2}(t)=O_{3}(t)=O_{4}(t)=0
. So
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}
,

\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=0-0=0
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=0-\mu_{5}=-\mu_{5}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})-h_{5}\mu_{5}<0
, which is satisfied obviously since
\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}<0
.

2.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)=0,\overline{Z}_{4}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{5}(t)>0

It is clear that server 1 serves queue 4, then
O_{4}(t)=\mu_{4}
,
O_{1}(t)=O_{3}(t)=0
. Thus,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-0>0
, which means that
t
is not differentiable.

3.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{5}(t)=0.

It is clear that server 1 serves queue 1, then
O_{1}(t)=\mu_{1}
,
O_{3}(t)=O_{4}(t)=0
, so
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}
,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=0-0=0
. Since
I_{5}(t)=O_{4}(t)=0
and
\overline{Z}_{5}(t)=0
, we have
O_{5}(t)=0
.
So
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=0-0=0
. It obviously holds that
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})<0
as required since
\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}<0
.

4.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)=0,\overline{Z}_{4}(t)=0,\overline{Z}_{5}(t)>0.

It is clear that server 2 serves queue 5, then
O_{5}(t)=\mu_{5}
,
O_{2}(t)=0
. To make
t
differentiable, we have
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-O_{1}(t)=0
, then
O_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}
and
\dot{\overline{T}}_{1}(t)=\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}}
.

If
\overline{Z}_{3}(t)=0
, since
I_{3}(t)=O_{2}(t)=0
, we have
O_{3}(t)=0
. Since
I_{4}(t)=O_{3}(t)=0
,
\overline{Z}_{4}(t)=0
, we have
O_{4}(t)=0
. Then
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=0-0=0
,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=0-\mu_{5}=-\mu_{5}
. It obviously holds that
\dot{Y}(t)=-h_{5}\mu_{5}<0
as required.

If
\overline{Z}_{3}(t)>0
, to keep
t
differentiable, we have
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=O_{3}(t)-O_{4}(t)=0
, which means
\mu_{3}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}}-\dot{\overline{T}}_{4}(t))=\mu_{4}\dot{\overline{T}}_{4}(t)\Leftrightarrow(\mu_{3}+\mu_{4})\dot{\overline{T}}_{4}(t)=\mu_{3}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})\Leftrightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{4}(t)=\frac{\mu_{3}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})}{\mu_{3}+\mu_{4}}
. Thus, if we want
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{5}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)<0
, we need
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=O_{4}(t)-\mu_{5}=\mu_{4}\dot{\overline{T}}_{4}(t)-\mu_{5}=\frac{\mu_{3}\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})}{\mu_{3}+\mu_{4}}-\mu_{5}<0
.
\frac{\mu_{3}\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})}{\mu_{3}+\mu_{4}}-\mu_{5}<0\Leftrightarrow 1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}}<\frac{\mu_{5}(\mu_{3}+\mu_{4})}{\mu_{3}\mu_{4}}\Leftrightarrow 1-m_{1}\alpha_{1}<\frac{m_{3}+m_{4}}{m_{5}}\Leftrightarrow m_{1}\alpha_{1}>\frac{m_{5}-m_{3}-m_{4}}{m_{5}}\Leftrightarrow\alpha_{1}>\frac{m_{5}-(1-m_{1})}{m_{1}m_{5}}\Leftrightarrow\alpha_{1}>\frac{m_{1}-m_{2}}{m_{1}m_{5}}
. Thus,
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{5}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)<0
is satisfied if
\alpha_{1}>\frac{m_{1}-m_{2}}{m_{1}m_{5}}
.

Note that
\frac{m_{1}-m_{2}}{m_{1}m_{5}}<1
, thus, the condition
\alpha_{1}>\frac{m_{1}-m_{2}}{m_{1}m_{5}}
does not conflict with the nominal condition(
\alpha_{1}<1
). It is clear that
\frac{m_{1}-m_{2}}{m_{1}m_{5}}<1
since
\frac{m_{1}-m_{2}}{m_{1}m_{5}}<1\Leftrightarrow m_{1}-m_{2}<m_{1}m_{5}\Leftrightarrow m_{2}>m_{1}(1-m_{5})\Leftrightarrow m_{2}>m_{1}m_{2}\Leftrightarrow m_{2}(1-m_{1})>0\Leftrightarrow m_{2}(m_{3}+m_{4})>0
, which is true obviously.

5.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)=0,\overline{Z}_{4}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{5}(t)=0.

It is clear that server 1 serves queue 4, then
O_{4}(t)=\mu_{4}
,
O_{1}(t)=O_{3}(t)=0
. Thus,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-0>0
, which means that
t
is not differentiable.

To sum up, except for the positive condition for common vector
h
, we need the following conditions from case 3:

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}>\frac{m_{1}-m_{2}}{m_{1}m_{5}}.

Case 4:
(3, 5)

1.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0.

It is clear that server 1 serves queue 1, server 2 serves queue 2, then
O_{1}(t)=\mu_{1}
,
O_{2}(t)=\mu_{2}
,
O_{3}(t)=O_{4}(t)=O_{5}(t)=0
. So
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}
,

\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=0-0=0
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})+h_{2}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})<0
.

2.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)=0,\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{4}(t)>0

It is clear that server 1 serves queue 4, then
O_{4}(t)=\mu_{4}
,
O_{1}(t)=O_{3}(t)=0
. Thus,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-0>0
, which means that
t
is not differentiable.

3.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{2}(t)=0.

It is clear that server 1 serves queue 1, then
O_{1}(t)=\mu_{1}
,
O_{4}(t)=O_{3}(t)=0
. Thus,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=0-0=0
,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}=0
. To make
t
differentiable, we have
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=\mu_{1}-O_{2}(t)=0
. It obviously holds that
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})<0
as required since
\alpha_{1}<\mu_{1}
.

4.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)=0,\overline{Z}_{2}(t)=0,\overline{Z}_{4}(t)>0.

It is clear that server 1 serves queue 4, then
O_{4}(t)=\mu_{4}
,
O_{1}(t)=O_{3}(t)=0
. Thus,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-0>0
, which means that
t
is not differentiable.

5.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)=0,\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{4}(t)=0.

It is clear that server 2 serves queue 2, then
O_{2}(t)=\mu_{2}
,
O_{5}(t)=0
. To make
t
differentiable, we have
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-O_{1}(t)=0
,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=O_{3}(t)-O_{4}(t)=0
, then
O_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}

\dot{\overline{T}}_{1}(t)=\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}}
,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{2}<0
,
O_{3}(t)=O_{4}(t)
. To have
O_{3}(t)=O_{4}(t)
, we need
\mu_{3}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}}-\dot{\overline{T}}_{4}(t))=\mu_{4}\dot{\overline{T}}_{4}(t)\Leftrightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{4}(t)=\frac{1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}}}{\mu_{3}+\mu_{4}}
, which is possible since
\dot{\overline{T}}_{4}(t)>0
. Thus, we have
\dot{Y}(t)<0
for sure.

To sum up, except for the positive condition for common vector
h
, we need the following condition from case 4:

\displaystyle h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})+h_{2}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})<0.

Case 5:
(4, 2)

1.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{5}(t)>0.

It is clear that server 1 serves queue 1, server 2 serves queue 5, then
O_{1}(t)=\mu_{1}
,
O_{5}(t)=\mu_{5}
,
O_{2}(t)=O_{3}(t)=O_{4}(t)=0
. So
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}
,

\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=0-0=0
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=0-\mu_{5}=-\mu_{5}
. It obviously holds that
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})-h_{5}\mu_{5}<0
as required since
\alpha_{1}<\mu_{1}
.

2.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)=0,\overline{Z}_{3}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{5}(t)>0

It is clear that server 2 serves queue 5, then
O_{5}(t)=\mu_{5}
,
O_{2}(t)=0
. Thus,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-0>0
, which means that
t
is not differentiable.

3.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{5}(t)=0.

It is clear that server 1 serves queue 1, then
O_{1}(t)=\mu_{1}
,
O_{3}(t)=O_{4}(t)=0
. So
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}
. Since
I_{5}(t)=O_{4}(t)=0
,
\overline{Z}_{5}(t)=0
, we have
O_{5}(t)=0
and then
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=0-0=0
.

If
\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0
, then server 2 serves queue 2, we have
O_{2}(t)=\mu_{2}
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=\mu_{2}-0=\mu_{2}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})+h_{3}\mu_{2}<0
.

If
\overline{Z}_{2}(t)=0
, when
\mu_{1}>\mu_{2}
,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}>0
and
t
is not differentiable; when
\mu_{1}\leq\mu_{2}
,
O_{2}(t)=\mu_{1}
,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=\mu_{1}-0=\mu_{1}
, and we need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})+h_{3}\mu_{1}<0
.

It is clear that when
\mu_{1}\leq\mu_{2}
,
h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})+h_{3}\mu_{2}\geq h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})+h_{3}\mu_{1}
. So it is sufficient to consider only
h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})+h_{3}\mu_{2}<0
in this situation.

4.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)=0,\overline{Z}_{3}(t)=0,\overline{Z}_{5}(t)>0.

It is clear that server 2 serves queue 5, then
O_{5}(t)=\mu_{5}
,
O_{2}(t)=0
. Since
I_{3}(t)=O_{2}(t)=0
,
\overline{Z}_{3}(t)=0
, we have
O_{3}(t)=0
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=0-0=0
. To keep
t
differentiable, we have
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-O_{1}(t)=0
, then
O_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}
and
\dot{\overline{T}}_{1}=\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}}
.

If
\overline{Z}_{4}(t)=0
, since
I_{4}(t)=O_{3}(t)=0
, we have
O_{4}(t)=0
. Then
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=0-\mu_{5}=-\mu_{5}
, It obviously holds that
\dot{Y}(t)=-h_{5}\mu_{5}<0
as required.

If
\overline{Z}_{4}(t)>0
, then
O_{4}(t)=\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})
, so
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)=\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})-\mu_{5}
. Thus, if we want
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{5}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)<0
, we need
\mu_{4}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})-\mu_{5}<0\Leftrightarrow 1-m_{1}\alpha_{1}<\frac{m_{4}}{m_{5}}\Leftrightarrow\alpha_{1}>\frac{m_{5}-m_{4}}{m_{1}m_{5}}
. Thus,
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{5}\dot{\overline{Z}}_{5}(t)<0
is satisfied if
\alpha_{1}>\frac{m_{5}-m_{4}}{m_{1}m_{5}}
.

Note that to make the condition
\alpha_{1}>\frac{m_{5}-m_{4}}{m_{1}m_{5}}
does not conflict with the nominal condition(
\alpha_{1}<1
), we need
\frac{m_{5}-m_{4}}{m_{1}m_{5}}<1\Leftrightarrow m_{5}-m_{4}<m_{1}m_{5}\Leftrightarrow m_{4}>m_{5}(1-m_{1})\Leftrightarrow m_{4}>m_{5}(m_{3}+m_{4})\Leftrightarrow m_{4}(1-m_{5})>m_{3}m_{5}\Leftrightarrow m_{2}m_{4}>m_{3}m_{5}
.

5.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)=0,\overline{Z}_{3}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{5}(t)=0.

It is clear that server 1 serves queue 3, then
O_{3}(t)=\mu_{3}
,
O_{1}(t)=O_{4}(t)=0
. Thus,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-0>0
, which means that
t
is not differentiable.

To sum up, except for the positive condition for common vector
h
, we need the following condition from case 5:

\displaystyle h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})+h_{3}\mu_{2}<0

\displaystyle m_{2}m_{4}>m_{4}m_{5},

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}>\frac{m_{5}-m_{4}}{m_{1}m_{5}}.

Case 6:
(4, 5)

1.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0.

It is clear that server 1 serves queue 1, server 2 serves queue 2, then
O_{1}(t)=\mu_{1}
,
O_{2}(t)=\mu_{2}
,
O_{3}(t)=O_{4}(t)=O_{5}(t)=0
. So
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}
,

\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=\mu_{2}-0=\mu_{2}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})+h_{2}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})+h_{3}\mu_{2}<0
.

2.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{2}(t)=0.

It is clear that server 1 serves queue 1, then
O_{1}(t)=\mu_{1}
,
O_{3}(t)=O_{4}(t)=0
. So
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}
. Since
O_{1}(t)=\mu_{1}
,
\overline{Z}_{2}(t)=0
, when
\mu_{1}>\mu_{2}
, we have
O_{2}(t)=\mu_{2}
, and then
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}>0
, so
t
is not differentiable; when
\mu_{1}\leq\mu_{2}
, we have
O_{2}(t)=\mu_{1}
, then
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=\mu_{1}-\mu_{1}=0
,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=\mu_{1}-0=\mu_{1}
, and we need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})+h_{3}\mu_{1}<0
(if
\mu_{1}\leq\mu_{2}
).

3.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)=0,\overline{Z}_{3}(t)>0

It is clear that server 1 serves queue 3, then
O_{3}(t)=\mu_{3}
,
O_{1}(t)=O_{4}(t)=0
. Thus,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-0>0
, which means that
t
is not differentiable.

4.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)=0,\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{3}(t)=0.

It is clear that server 2 serves queue 2, then
O_{2}(t)=\mu_{2}
,
O_{5}(t)=0
. To make
t
differentiable, we have
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-O_{1}(t)=0
, then
O_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}
,
\dot{\overline{T}}_{1}(t)=\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}}
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{2}
. Thus, the maximum of
O_{3}(t)=\mu_{3}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})
. When
\mu_{2}>\mu_{3}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})
, we have
O_{3}(t)=\mu_{3}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})
, and then
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=\mu_{2}-\mu_{3}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})>0
, so
t
is not differentiable; when
\mu_{2}\leq\mu_{3}(1-\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\mu_{1}})
, we have
O_{3}(t)=\mu_{2}
, then
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=\mu_{2}-\mu_{2}=0
, and it obviously holds that
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{2}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{2})<0
as required since
\alpha_{1}<\mu_{2}
.

To sum up, except for the positive condition for common vector
h
, we need the following condition from case 6:

\displaystyle h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})+h_{2}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})+h_{3}\mu_{2}<0

\displaystyle h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})+h_{3}\mu_{1}<0\;\;\text{if}\;\;\mu_{1}\leq\mu_{2}.

Appendix D
Detailed Proofs for Balanced Lu-Kumar Networks

D.1
Chen-
\mathcal{S}
in Balanced Lu-Kumar Networks

In this appendix, we prove that the reflection matrices for the four static-priority polices of a balanced Lu-Kumar network (
m_{1}+m_{4}=1
;
m_{2}+m_{3}=1
) with
\alpha_{1}<1
are Chen-
\mathcal{S}
, invertible and have same-sign determinants if
m_{2}+m_{4}<1
. Theorem
4
then guarantees that the refection matrix is invertible and Chen-
\mathcal{S}
for any ratio matrix
\Delta
of a balanced Lu-Kumar network if
m_{2}+m_{4}<1
.

Given a static priority ratio matrix
\Delta
, to prove that the corresponding
(R_{\Delta},\theta_{\Delta})
is Chen-
\mathcal{S}
, we must show that (i)
R
is completely-
\mathcal{S}
, and (ii) that there exists a positive vector
h\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
such that (
2.8
) satisfies for each of the 3 partitions of
\mathcal{J}
.

Proof.

Proof for priority 1, 2 (Chen-S).

In the balanced Lu-Kumar network with class 1 having the priority in station 1 and class 2 having the priority in station 2, we have
J=2
,
K=4
,
\rho=\left[\alpha_{1}\ \alpha_{1}\right]^{\prime},

R=\left[\begin{matrix}1&-\displaystyle\frac{m_{4}}{m_{3}}\mcr 0&1\end{matrix}\right],

\displaystyle\theta=R(\rho-e)=(\alpha_{1}-1)\left[\begin{matrix}1-\displaystyle\frac{m_{4}}{m_{3}}\mcr 1\end{matrix}\right].

It is clear that the reflection matrix
R
is completely-
\mathcal{S}
, since
R
is
2\times 2
,
R^{-1}=CMQ\Delta>0
and all its diagonal elements are positive.

Partition 1:
a=1;b=2

\theta_{b}=\theta_{2}=\alpha_{1}-1,\;R_{b}^{-1}=R_{2}^{-1}=1\Rightarrow u=-R_{b}^{-1}\theta_{b}=1-\alpha_{1}\Rightarrow R_{ab}u=(\alpha_{1}-1)\displaystyle\frac{m_{4}}{m_{3}}.

Thus,
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-1)
. Since
\alpha_{1}<1
, taking
h_{1}>0
, we ensure
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]<0
.

Partition 2:
a=2;b=1

\theta_{b}=\theta_{1}=(\alpha_{1}-1)(1-\displaystyle\frac{m_{4}}{m_{3}}),\;R_{b}^{-1}=R_{1}^{-1}=1\Rightarrow u=-R_{b}^{-1}\theta_{b}=(1-\alpha_{1})\left(1-\displaystyle\frac{m_{4}}{m_{3}}\right);

R_{ab}=0\Rightarrow R_{ab}u=0.

Thus,
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]=h_{2}(\alpha_{1}-1)
. Since
\alpha_{1}<1
, taking
h_{2}>0
, we ensure
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]<0
.

Partition 3:
a=1,2;b=\varnothing

h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]=h^{\prime}\theta=(\alpha_{1}-1)\left[\left(1-\displaystyle\frac{m_{4}}{m_{3}}\right)h_{1}+h_{2}\right].

Since
\alpha_{1}<1
, fixing
h_{2}>0
large enough, we guarantee that
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]<0
.

We have shown, then, that exists a positive vector
h\in{R^{2}}
as required so that
(R,\theta)
under the static-priority policy 1, 2 is Chen-
\mathcal{S}
.
∎

∎

Proof.

Proof for priority 1, 3 (Chen-S).

In the balanced Lu-Kumar network with class 1 having the priority in station 1 and class 3 having the priority in station 2, we have
J=2,K=4,\rho=\left[\alpha_{1}\ \alpha_{1}\right]^{\prime},

R=\left[\begin{matrix}1&-m_{4}\mcr 0&m_{2}\end{matrix}\right],

\displaystyle\theta=R(\rho-e)=(\alpha_{1}-1)\left[\begin{matrix}m_{1}\mcr m_{2}\end{matrix}\right].

It is clear that the reflection matrix
R
is completely-
\mathcal{S}
, since
R
is
2\times 2
,
R^{-1}=CMQ\Delta>0
and all its diagonal elements are positive.

Partition 1:
a=1;b=2

\theta_{b}=\theta_{2}=(\alpha_{1}-1)m_{2},\;R_{b}^{-1}=R_{2}^{-1}=\displaystyle\frac{1}{m_{2}}\Rightarrow u=-R_{b}^{-1}\theta_{b}=1-\alpha_{1}\Rightarrow R_{ab}u=(\alpha_{1}-1)m_{4}.

Thus,
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-1)
. Since
\alpha_{1}<1
, taking
h_{1}>0
, we ensure
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]<0
.

Partition 2:
a=2;b=1

\theta_{b}=\theta_{1}=(\alpha_{1}-1)m_{1},\;R_{b}^{-1}=R_{1}^{-1}=1\Rightarrow u=-R_{b}^{-1}\theta_{b}=(1-\alpha_{1})m_{1};

R_{ab}=0\Rightarrow R_{ab}u=0.

Thus,
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]=h_{2}(\alpha_{1}-1)m_{2}
. Since
\alpha_{1}<1
, taking
h_{2}>0
, we ensure
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]<0
.

Partition 3:
a=1,2;b=\varnothing

h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]=h^{\prime}\theta=(\alpha_{1}-1)[m_{1}h_{1}+m_{2}h_{2}]

Since
\alpha_{1}<1
, taking
h_{1}>0
,
h_{2}>0
, we ensure
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]<0
.

We have shown, then, that exists a positive vector
h\in{R^{2}}
as required so that
(R,\theta)
under the static-priority policy 1, 3 is Chen-
\mathcal{S}
.
∎

∎

Proof.

Proof for priority 4,2 (Chen-S).

In the balanced Lu-Kumar network with class 4 having the priority in station 1 and class 2 having the priority in station 2, we have
J=2,K=4,\rho=\left[\alpha_{1}\ \alpha_{1}\right]^{\prime},

R=\displaystyle\frac{1}{m_{1}-m_{2}}\left[\begin{matrix}m_{1}m_{3}&-m_{1}m_{4}\mcr-m_{3}&m_{3}\end{matrix}\right],

\displaystyle\theta=R(\rho-e)=(\alpha_{1}-1)\left[\begin{matrix}m_{1}\mcr 0\end{matrix}\right].

It is clear that the reflection matrix
R
is completely-
\mathcal{S}
if
m_{1}-m_{2}>0
, equivalently
m_{2}+m_{4}<1
, since
R
is
2\times 2
,
R^{-1}=CMQ\Delta>0
and all its diagonal elements are positive when
m_{1}-m_{2}>0
.

Partition 1:
a=1;b=2

\theta_{b}=\theta_{2}=0,\;R_{b}^{-1}=R_{2}^{-1}=\displaystyle\frac{m_{1}-m_{2}}{m_{3}}\Rightarrow u=-R_{b}^{-1}\theta_{b}=0;

R_{ab}=\frac{m_{1}m_{4}}{m_{2}-m_{1}}\Rightarrow R_{ab}u=0.

Thus,
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-1)m_{1}
. Since
\alpha_{1}<1
, taking
h_{1}>0
, we ensure
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]<0
.

Partition 2:
a=2;b=1

\theta_{b}=\theta_{1}=(\alpha_{1}-1)m_{1},\;R_{b}^{-1}=R_{1}^{-1}=\frac{m_{1}-m_{2}}{m_{1}m_{3}}\Rightarrow u=-R_{b}^{-1}\theta_{b}=(\alpha_{1}-1)\frac{m_{2}-m_{1}}{m_{3}};

R_{ab}=\frac{m_{3}}{m_{2}-m_{1}}\Rightarrow R_{ab}u=\alpha_{1}-1.

Thus,
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]=h_{2}(\alpha_{1}-1)
. Since
\alpha_{1}<1
, taking
h_{2}>0
, we ensure
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]<0
.

Partition 3:
a=1,2;b=\varnothing

h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]=h^{\prime}\theta=(\alpha_{1}-1)m_{1}h_{1}

Since
\alpha_{1}<1
, taking
h_{1}>0
, we ensure
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]<0
.

We have shown, then, that exists a positive vector
h\in{R^{2}}
as required so that
(R,\theta)
under the static-priority policy 4, 2 is Chen-
\mathcal{S}
.
∎

∎

Proof.

Proof for priority 4, 3 (Chen-S).

In the balanced Lu-Kumar network with class 4 having priority in station 1 and class 3 having priority in station 2, we have
J=2
,
K=4
,
\rho=\left[\alpha_{1}\ \alpha_{1}\right]^{\prime},

R=\left[\begin{matrix}1&-m_{4}\mcr-\displaystyle\frac{m_{2}}{m_{1}}&\displaystyle\frac{m_{2}}{m_{1}}\end{matrix}\right],

\displaystyle\theta=R(\rho-e)=(\alpha_{1}-1)\left[\begin{matrix}m_{1}\mcr 0\end{matrix}\right].

It is clear that the reflection matrix
R
is completely-
\mathcal{S}
, since
R
is
2\times 2
,
R^{-1}=CMQ\Delta>0
and all its diagonal elements are positive.

Partition 1:
a=1;b=2

\theta_{b}=\theta_{2}=0,\;R_{b}^{-1}=R_{2}^{-1}=\displaystyle\frac{m_{1}}{m_{2}}\Rightarrow u=0;

R_{ab}=-m_{4}\Rightarrow R_{ab}u=0.

Thus,
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-1)m_{1}
. Since
\alpha_{1}<1
, taking
h_{1}>0
, we ensure
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]<0
.

Partition 2:
a=2;b=1

\theta_{b}=\theta_{1}=(\alpha_{1}-1)m_{1},\;R_{b}^{-1}=R_{1}^{-1}=1\Rightarrow u=-R_{b}^{-1}\theta_{b}=(1-\alpha_{1})m_{1};

R_{ab}=-\frac{m_{2}}{m_{1}}\Rightarrow R_{ab}u=(\alpha_{1}-1)m_{2}.

Thus,
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]=h_{2}(\alpha_{1}-1)m_{2}
. Since
\alpha_{1}<1
, taking
h_{2}>0
, we ensure
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]<0
.

Partition 3:
a=1,2;b=\varnothing

h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]=h^{\prime}\theta=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-1)m_{1}

Since
\alpha_{1}<1
, taking
h_{1}>0
, we ensure
h_{a}^{{}^{\prime}}\left[\theta_{a}+R_{ab}u\right]<0
.

We have shown, then, that exists a positive vector
h\in{R^{2}}
as required so that
(R,\theta)
under the static-priority policy 4, 3 is Chen-
\mathcal{S}
.
∎

∎

We can now complete the proof that the 4 static-priority cases are all Chen-
\mathcal{S}
if
m_{2}+m_{4}<1
.

The reflection matrices for the 4 static-priorty policies are invertible. We can calculate that
det(R_{1,2})=1>0
,
det(R_{1,3})=m_{2}>0
,
det(R_{4,2})=\displaystyle\frac{m_{1}m_{3}}{1-m_{4}-m_{2}}
,
det(R_{4,3})=m_{2}>0
, where the subscript label indicates the classes with the priority in each station. These determinants have the same sign if
m_{2}+m_{4}<1
. We can conclude that the reflection matrix for a balanced push started Lu-Kumar network is invertible for any ratio matrix
\Delta
provided that
m_{2}+m_{4}<1
.

According to Theorem
4
, in a balanced Lu-Kumar network with
\alpha_{1}<1
, the reflection matrix is invertible and
(R,\theta)
is Chen-
\mathcal{S}
for any ratio matrix
\Delta
if
m_{2}+m_{4}<1
.

D.2
The SSC Inequalities for Balanced Lu-Kumar Networks

In balanced Lu-Kumar networks, we have
m_{1}+m_{4}=m_{2}+m_{3}=1
. We consider the 4 static priority policies of balanced Lu-Kumar networks for (
B.1
) in the following analysis.

Given a policy
\pi
, we let
\dot{Y}^{\pi}(t)=\sum_{k\in\mathcal{H}_{\pi}:z_{k}>0}h_{k}\dot{\overline{Z}^{\pi}_{k}}(t)
. We need
\dot{Y}^{\pi}(t)<0
with a common vector
h\in\mathbb{R}^{K}_{++}
for any regular time
t
with
\lVert\overline{Z}^{\pi}_{\mathcal{H}_{\pi}}(t)\rVert>0
, i.e., with at least one non-empty high-priority queue. We omit
\pi
when it is fixed. In the balanced Lu-Kumar network,
m_{k}<1
and thus
\mu_{k}>1
for all
k\in\mathcal{K}
. Denote
I_{k}(t)
as the input rate of class
k
at time
t
;
O_{k}(t)
as the output rate of class
k
at time
t
. Then,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{k}(t)=I_{k}(t)-O_{k}(t)\text{ for }k\in\mathcal{K};I_{k}(t)=O_{k-1}(t)\text{ for }k\in\mathcal{K}/\{1\};I_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}
.

We use the set of high priority classes to represent static priority policies; (1,2) for example stands for a static priority policy that class 1 and 2 have the priorities in their respective stations.

Case 1:
(4, 2)

1.

\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{4}(t)>0.

In this case, by the priority policy, at time
t
server 1 serves queue 4 and server 2 serves queue 2 so that
O_{4}(t)=\mu_{4}
,
O_{2}(t)=\mu_{2}
,
O_{1}(t)=O_{3}(t)=0
. Then,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=0-\mu_{2}=-\mu_{2}
and

\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=0-\mu_{4}=-\mu_{4}
. It obviously holds that
\dot{Y}(t)=-h_{2}\mu_{2}-h_{4}\mu_{4}<0
as required.

2.

\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{4}(t)=0

In this scenario, server 2 serves queue 2 so that
O_{2}(t)=\mu_{2}
,
O_{3}(t)=0
. Since
I_{4}(t)=O_{3}(t)=0
and
\overline{Z}_{4}(t)=0
, we have
O_{4}(t)=0
.
We have then that
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=0-0=0
. If
\overline{Z}_{1}(t)=0
,
O_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}
,

\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{2}<0
, thus, we have
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{2}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{2})<0
. If
\overline{Z}_{1}(t)>0
,
O_{1}(t)=\mu_{1}
,

\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}
and
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{2}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})<0
, if
\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}<0
, equivalently,
m_{2}<m_{1}\Leftrightarrow m_{2}+m_{4}<1
.

3.

\overline{Z}_{2}(t)=0,\overline{Z}_{4}(t)>0.

It is clear that server 1 serves queue 4, then
O_{4}(t)=\mu_{4}
and
O_{1}(t)=0
. Since
\overline{Z}_{2}(t)=0
,
O_{2}(t)=0
,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=0-0=0
. If
\overline{Z}_{3}(t)=0
, then since
O_{2}(t)=0
, we have
O_{3}(t)=0
and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=0-\mu_{4}=-\mu_{4}<0
, thus, it obviously holds that
\dot{Y}(t)=-\mu_{4}h_{4}<0
as required. If
\overline{Z}_{3}(t)>0
, then
O_{3}(t)=\mu_{3}
, and
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=\mu_{3}-\mu_{4}
, thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)=(\mu_{3}-\mu_{4})h_{4}<0
, which is satisfied only when
\mu_{3}-\mu_{4}<0
, equivalently,
m_{4}<m_{3}\Leftrightarrow m_{2}+m_{4}<1
.

We conclude that, in addition to the positivity of
h
, the condition
m_{2}+m_{4}<1
arises from case 1.

Case 2:
(1, 2)

1.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0.

In this scenario, server 1 serves queue 1, server 2 serves queue 2, so that
O_{1}(t)=\mu_{1}
,
O_{2}(t)=\mu_{2}
,
I_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}
and, in turn,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}<0
and

\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})+h_{2}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})<0
.

2.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{2}(t)=0.

In this scenario, server 1 serves queue 1 which it prioritizes so that then
O_{1}(t)=\mu_{1}
,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}<0
. If
\mu_{1}>\mu_{2}
, then since
\overline{Z}_{2}(t)=0
,
O_{2}(t)=\mu_{2}
,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}>0
, which means that
t
is not differentiable. If
\mu_{1}\leq\mu_{2}
, then since
\overline{Z}_{2}(t)=0
,
O_{2}(t)=\mu_{1}
,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=\mu_{1}-\mu_{1}=0
.
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}<0
. Thus, it obviously holds that
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})<0
as required.

3.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)=0,\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0.

It is clear that
\alpha_{1}<\mu_{1}
, since
\overline{Z}_{1}(t)=0
, we have
O_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}
, then
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{1}=0
. Since
\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0
, server 2 serves queue 2, we have
O_{2}(t)=\mu_{2}
, then
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{2}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{2}<0
. It obviously holds that
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{2}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{2})<0
as required.

To sum up, in addition to the positivity of
h
, we need the following conditions from case 2:

\displaystyle h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})+h_{2}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})<0.

Note that when
m_{2}+m_{4}<1\Leftrightarrow\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}<0
(from Case 1), we have
h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})+h_{2}(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})<0
, thus, we do not need to consider any additional conditions from Case 2.

Case 3:
(1, 3)

1.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{3}(t)>0.

In this case,
I_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}
, server 1 serves queue 1, server 2 serves queue 3, then
O_{1}(t)=\mu_{1}
,
O_{3}(t)=\mu_{3}
,
O_{2}(t)=O_{4}(t)=0
. So
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}<0
and

\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=0-\mu_{3}=-\mu_{3}
. Thus, it obviously holds that
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})-h_{3}\mu_{3}<0
as required.

2.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{3}(t)=0.

It is clear that server 1 serves queue 1, then
O_{1}(t)=\mu_{1}
,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1}<0
. If
O_{2}(t)\geq\mu_{3}
, we have
O_{3}(t)=\mu_{3}
since queue 3 has priority in station 2, then
\dot{\overline{T}}_{3}(t)=1\Rightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{2}(t)=0\Rightarrow O_{2}(t)=0
, which is conflict with
O_{2}(t)\geq\mu_{3}
. As a result,
O_{2}(t)<\mu_{3}
, and then
O_{3}(t)=O_{2}(t)
,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=O_{2}(t)-O_{3}(t)=0
. It obviously holds that
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{1}(\alpha_{1}-\mu_{1})<0
as required.

3.

\overline{Z}_{1}(t)=0,\overline{Z}_{3}(t)>0.

In this scenario, server 2 serves queue 3, then
O_{3}(t)=\mu_{3}
,
O_{2}(t)=0
, so
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=0-\mu_{3}=-\mu_{3}
. Since
I_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}<\mu_{1}
and
\overline{Z}_{1}(t)=0
, we have
O_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}
.
So
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{1}(t)=\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{1}=0
. It obviously holds that
\dot{Y}(t)=-\mu_{3}h_{3}<0
as required.

To sum up, except for the positive condition for common vector
h
, we do not need any other conditions from Case 3.

Case 4:
(4, 3)

1.

\overline{Z}_{3}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{4}(t)>0.

It is clear that server 1 serves queue 4, server 2 serves queue 3, then
O_{4}(t)=\mu_{4}
,
O_{3}(t)=\mu_{3}
,
O_{1}(t)=O_{2}(t)=0
. So
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=0-\mu_{3}=\mu_{3}
and

\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=\mu_{3}-\mu_{4}
. Thus, we need
\dot{Y}(t)=-\mu_{3}h_{3}+h_{4}(\mu_{3}-\mu_{4})<0
.

2.

\overline{Z}_{3}(t)=0,\overline{Z}_{4}(t)>0.

In this case, server 1 serves queue 4, then
O_{4}(t)=\mu_{4}
,
O_{1}(t)=0
. If
\overline{Z}_{2}(t)=0
, since
O_{1}(t)=0
, we have
O_{2}(t)=0
, then
O_{3}(t)=0
since
\overline{Z}_{3}(t)=0
, thus,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=0-0=0
and

\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=0-\mu_{4}=\mu_{4}
, and it obviously holds that
\dot{Y}(t)=-h_{4}\mu_{4}<0
as required. If
\overline{Z}_{2}(t)>0
, to make
t
differentiable, we have
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=O_{2}(t)-O_{3}(t)=0\Leftrightarrow\mu_{2}\dot{\overline{T}}_{2}(t)-\mu_{3}\dot{\overline{T}}_{3}(t)=0\Leftrightarrow\mu_{2}\dot{\overline{T}}_{2}(t)-\mu_{3}(1-\dot{\overline{T}}_{2}(t))=0\Leftrightarrow(\mu_{2}+\mu_{3})\dot{\overline{T}}_{2}(t)=\mu_{3}\Leftrightarrow\dot{\overline{T}}_{2}(t)=\displaystyle\frac{\mu_{3}}{\mu_{2}+\mu_{3}}=\displaystyle\frac{\frac{1}{m_{3}}}{\frac{1}{m_{2}}+\frac{1}{m_{3}}}=\displaystyle\frac{m_{2}}{m_{3}+m_{2}}=m_{2}
. Thus,
O_{3}(t)=O_{2}(t)=\mu_{2}\dot{\overline{T}}_{2}(t)=1
,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=1-\mu_{4}<0
, and it obviously holds that
\dot{Y}(t)=h_{4}(1-\mu_{4})<0
as required.

3.

\overline{Z}_{3}(t)>0,\overline{Z}_{4}(t)=0.

In this scenario, server 2 serves queue 3, then
O_{3}(t)=\mu_{3}
,
O_{2}(t)=0
. Thus,
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{3}(t)=0-\mu_{3}=\mu_{3}
. To make
t
differentiable, we have
\dot{\overline{Z}}_{4}(t)=\mu_{3}-O_{4}(t)=0
. It obviously holds that
\dot{Y}(t)=-h_{3}\mu_{3}<0
as required.

To sum up, except for the positive condition for common vector
h
, we need the following condition from Case 4:

\displaystyle-\mu_{3}h_{3}+h_{4}(\mu_{3}-\mu_{4})<0.

Note that when
m_{2}+m_{4}<1\Leftrightarrow m_{4}<m_{3}\Leftrightarrow\mu_{3}-\mu_{4}<0
(from Case 1), we have
-\mu_{3}h_{3}+h_{4}(\mu_{3}-\mu_{4})<0
, thus, we do not need to consider any additional conditions from Case 4.
